## 🐛 fix(cluster): presence age on this node's clock; drain key and duplicate cleanup (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/cluster-presence-clock`)

### What changed and why

**Presence ages are measured on this node's own clock (OPS-1).** The presence watch stamped each
record with the NATS server's write time and expired it against this node's wall clock after the
30 s bucket TTL. A node whose clock ran about 20 s ahead therefore dropped every member between two
rewrites, and `members()`, `currentMembers()` and the RPC peer list came back empty. Four staleness
checks compared another node's `updatedAt` with this node's clock against two presence intervals
(20 s): the drain `LAST_NODE` guard, the drained node's own check of a drain request over RPC, the
overview's `STALE` card, and the watcher's `node.stale` entry. Skew above about 10 s was enough to
refuse a legitimate drain or to report a live node as stale.

`ClusterPresence` now records, for each record, the revision it saw and when it saw that revision on
its monotonic clock. Expiry uses that time, and the new `heartbeatAgeMillis(nodeId)` reports it.
The four checks use that age. A record that was already in the bucket when this node started looking
(the watch's initial values, the first bucket read, or a read more than one TTL after the previous
one) was written at an unknown moment, so it has no age until it is rewritten. The drain guards count
such a node as not serving, so they refuse rather than drain the last live node. The overview shows
it `LIVE` with the age its own timestamp gives, and the watcher does not report it. `updatedAt`
stays in the record and in the overview as `lastHeartbeat`, for display only. `node.stale` is still
keyed by `updatedAt`, so every node's report of one stale record collapses into one entry.

**The drain key of a decision being applied survives the node's own key sync (OPS-9).** `drain()`
opens `gate.drain`, writes `drain.<node>` and then asks the node over RPC. If the node's scheduled
`syncOwnDrainKey` ran in between, it saw a key for a node that was not draining yet and deleted it.
The node then refused the request as "not recorded", and the administrator got `409 NODE_FAILED`. The
sync now reads the key's revision first, then skips the delete while the drain gate is held, and
deletes only at that revision. A key written after the read is either covered by a gate the check
sees or carries a newer revision, and the conditional delete then fails. The drain flag is read
again just before the delete, so a drain applied on the node while the sync ran — the gate already
closed again after the reply — keeps its key too. A leftover key from a drain that a restart ended
is still removed on the next sync.

**Dead code (OPS-18).** `ClusterPresence.duplicateDetail()` had no caller; the same detail is in the
ERROR log. `KvLeaseManager.snapshot(prefix, max)` listed `max * 2 + 16` entries before filtering by
prefix, so a real prefix could miss matching leases. Its only production caller passed `""`, so it is
now `snapshot(max)`. `heartbeatAll()` is kept: nine tests use it.

**Tests (OPS-20, the presence part).** Skewed-clock tests for presence expiry and heartbeat age, the
drain guards, the overview and the watcher. The tests of the drain-key sync include one where the
key is rewritten between the sync's read and its delete.

**Files:** [`ClusterPresence.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/ClusterPresence.java),
[`ClusterAdminService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/admin/ClusterAdminService.java),
[`ClusterWatcher.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/admin/ClusterWatcher.java),
[`KvLeaseManager.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/lease/KvLeaseManager.java),
[`coordinator-admin.md`](../coordinator-admin.md)

## 🐛 fix(conversations): the chat panel resumes only the caller's own conversation (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/conversations-mine-flag`)

### What changed and why

The Manager's chat panel opens an agent by resuming the newest `READY` or `AWAITING_HUMAN` conversation
the listing returns. An administrator's listing holds **every user's** conversations, so an admin who
opened an agent an end user had talked to more recently landed in that user's conversation, and the
next message ran in it as that user: their tools, property writes and long-term memory. The admin
passes the per-conversation ownership check, so nothing stopped it. The operator's "restore my last
investigation" had the same shape: a fresh tab restored another admin's newest operator conversation,
pending approval card included, and its History list showed and opened other admins' investigations.

The Manager cannot filter this by itself: a conversation's owner is the principal name the backend
resolved, which the Manager does not reliably know. So the listing gained a flag:

- **`GET /conversationstore/conversations?mine=true`** lists only the caller's own conversations, for
  every caller: an administrator, and an agent's reviewer (who otherwise also sees that agent's
  conversations). The owner is pushed into the descriptor query as an exact match on `userId`, so a
  pre-v5.1.6 conversation that records no owner on its descriptor is left out, and the per-row check
  enforces the same, as it stays the authority over the query. The "search matches nothing, list
  everything" fallback is decided within the same set. Without a named caller, or with authorization
  disabled (a conversation then records the userId the client passed, not the principal, even when a
  token names the caller), there is no owner and the flag changes nothing. The default is `false`: existing clients are unchanged.
- **Manager:** the chat panel's resume and its history list (they share one cache entry, so they must
  ask the same question), the operator's restore and its History list all ask for `mine`. Browsing
  everybody's conversations stays on the Conversations page.
- **Operator polling:** the 1.5 s poll while a resumed turn settles slept through a helper that added an
  abort listener per call and never removed it, about 200 on one signal over the five-minute ceiling.
  It is now `lib/abortable-sleep.ts`, which removes its listener when the timer fires.
- **Operator History** sorts with `conversationRecency`, whose comment already claimed the list shared
  it; the list carried its own copy of the expression.

**Files:** [`IRestConversationStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/rest/IRestConversationStore.java),
[`RestConversationStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/rest/RestConversationStore.java),
[`conversations.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/api/conversations.ts),
[`use-chat.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/hooks/use-chat.ts),
[`use-operator-chat.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/hooks/use-operator-chat.ts),
[`use-conversations.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/hooks/use-conversations.ts),
[`operator-history.tsx`](../../ui/manager/src/components/operator/operator-history.tsx),
[`abortable-sleep.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/abortable-sleep.ts),
[`rest-api-reference.md`](../rest-api-reference.md#the-conversation-store)

### Design decisions

- A server-side flag, not a client filter: only the backend knows the principal name it stamps as owner.
- Exact owner match, no "or unowned" alternative: a legacy conversation without a recorded owner is not
  "mine" to resume. Same semantics as the open "search my conversations" endpoint (#1307).
- The old eight-argument Java signature stays as a `default` overload (`mine=false`), so the MCP tool and
  the existing tests are unchanged.

## 🐛 fix(hitl): crash recovery also recovers interrupted group discussions (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/group-crash-recovery`)

### What changed and why

A group discussion whose node died mid-run stayed `IN_PROGRESS` (or `SYNTHESIZING`) for good. Its leg lives only in the JVM that ran it, the startup crash recovery deliberately skipped running group discussions, and `continue` and `followup` both require `COMPLETED`, so nothing but a manual cancel could end it. The sharpest case was a crash right after an approve: the approve CAS clears the pause bookmark in the same write that moves the discussion to `IN_PROGRESS`, so there was no pause left to restore either. Only a standing team's run had a backstop, the cadence claim TTL, whose default is 24 hours.

- **`HitlCrashRecoveryObserver`** — the single-node startup pass now scans `IN_PROGRESS` and `SYNTHESIZING` group discussions and cancels each one last written before the JVM started, through `IGroupConversationService.cancelDiscussion` (the ordinary cancel: state CAS, timeout schedule removed). It is gated on `eddi.hitl.crash-recovery.recover-in-progress`, like the regular IN_PROGRESS recovery, and the WARN summary counts the cancelled discussions. The first start after the upgrade also cancels every discussion that was already stuck this way. A running-state cancel releases no ephemeral agents (no leg is left to); they go when the discussion is closed or deleted. The leader-elected cluster sweep (`runClusterRecovery`) is unchanged and does not cancel groups.
- **`GroupHitlCoordinator.resumeDiscussion`** — the approve resume now stamps `lastModified`, as the human-turn and tie-break resumes already did. Without it, a discussion paused before a restart and approved after it would carry the pre-restart timestamp into its new, live leg, and the recovery pass could cancel that leg.
- **Docs** — [`hitl.md`](../hitl.md#crash-recovery) (crash recovery list, overlapping-JVM caveat), a new "Restarts" section in [`group-conversations.md`](../group-conversations.md#restarts), and the `recover-in-progress` row of [`configuration-reference.md`](../configuration-reference.md).

### Design decisions

- **Process start, not a timestamp taken in the observer.** The cut-off is the JVM start time (`RuntimeMXBean.getStartTime()`): nothing this process runs can have been written before it, whatever ran before the `StartupEvent` observer.
- **Cancel, not restore.** A resumed group leg keeps no bookmark, so there is no pause to put back; cancelling ends in a state the user can act on, and a standing team's next fire returns its pulled backlog tasks to `PENDING`.
- **Reuse `cancelDiscussion`** rather than a raw state write, so the transition is the CAS-guarded one a manual cancel makes.
- **Single node only.** In cluster mode a group discussion holds no conversation lease that could prove its leg dead; a cluster variant would need its own staleness rule and is left as a follow-up.

**Files:** [`HitlCrashRecoveryObserver.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/hitl/HitlCrashRecoveryObserver.java), [`GroupHitlCoordinator.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/groups/GroupHitlCoordinator.java), [`HitlCrashRecoveryObserverTest.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/hitl/HitlCrashRecoveryObserverTest.java), [`GroupHitlCoordinatorTest.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/groups/GroupHitlCoordinatorTest.java)

## 🐛 fix(groups): streaming continue binds the caller; MCP and templates enforce REST's checks (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/group-rest-mcp-parity`)

### What changed and why

Three places where one surface of the group API did less than its sibling.

- **A streamed continuation ran without its caller.**
  `POST /groups/{groupId}/conversations/{id}/continue/stream` hands the round to
  `RestGroupConversation`'s own virtual-thread executor. Nothing carried the caller
  across, and on that thread the request scope is gone, so the member turns'
  `captureOrCurrent()` found nobody: every `${caller:userId}` and `${caller:token}`
  failed closed on this path alone. The caller is now captured on the request
  thread and bound around the work with `CallerIdentityContext.withIdentity`, the
  way `startAndDiscussAsync` already does for a streamed start. `CallerIdentityContext` is a new constructor
  parameter of `RestGroupConversation`.
- **The MCP discussion tools skipped the question ceilings.** REST re-checks by
  hand that a question is present and at most 50,000 characters, and that `userId`
  and `targetAgentId` are at most 256, because a question is fanned out to every
  member in every phase at the group owner's cost. An MCP tool calls the service
  in-process, where no Bean Validation constraint runs, so a caller with the
  CONVERSE role (`eddi-viewer` included) could send a multi-megabyte question, or
  start a discussion about nothing. The check now lives in `GroupQuestionLimits`,
  next to `GroupAttachmentLimits`, and `discuss_with_group`,
  `start_group_discussion`, `followup_with_member` and `continue_group_discussion`
  call it before any read, access check or service call, answering
  `{"error": …, "errorCode": "BAD_REQUEST"}`. REST calls the same class, so both
  surfaces refuse the same input with the same text. The constants stay on
  `IRestGroupConversation`.
- **Creating a group from a template turned an access refusal into a 500.**
  `POST /groupstore/templates/{id}/instantiate` saves through
  `IRestAgentGroupStore.createGroup`, which throws `ForbiddenException` for an
  assigned agent the caller may not use. The generic `catch (Exception)` logged it
  as an error with a stack trace and answered `500`. A `ForbiddenException` or a
  JAX-RS `WebApplicationException` is now rethrown, as `RestTeamHandoffs` and
  `RestGroupWorkspace` do, so the caller gets the `403` (or the store's own status).
  The MCP side had the same fault: `create_group_from_template`, and every other
  group tool that ends in `McpGroupTools.failure` (`create_group`, `update_group`
  and the rest), answered the store's `ForbiddenException` with an `INTERNAL`
  reference and a logged stack trace. `failure` now answers it with
  `"errorCode": "FORBIDDEN"` and the guard's text, through the shared
  `McpToolUtils.accessRefusal` that `toolFailure` already uses.

The Javadoc on `budgetRefused` said the team-budget refusal "carries no
caller-supplied id". It does carry the group id: the stored one, already resolved
by the group read that precedes admission, and needed by the administrator `PUT`
path in the exhausted message. The comment now says that.

### Design decisions

- **Bind at the dispatch, not in the service.** Moving the async dispatch into
  `GroupConversationService`, or running the SSE method inline on a virtual
  thread, would also fix it, but would change when the SSE response is committed.
  Capturing before `submit` is the established pattern and a two-line change.
- **One check class, not a public static on the REST resource.** The MCP tools
  are in another package; making `RestGroupConversation.rejectionReason` public
  would have the MCP layer depend on a JAX-RS resource. `GroupQuestionLimits`
  reads the ceilings from `IRestGroupConversation`, so nothing is duplicated.
- **List limits not clamped.** The plan also suggested clamping `limit` in
  `list_groups` and `list_group_conversations`. REST takes the same unbounded
  `limit`, and both are bounded by the storage layer's `MAX_RESULT_LIMIT` (10,000),
  so this is not a check REST makes and MCP skips. Left for a change that bounds
  both surfaces together.

**Files:** [`RestGroupConversation.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/RestGroupConversation.java),
[`GroupQuestionLimits.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/groups/GroupQuestionLimits.java),
[`McpGroupTools.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/mcp/McpGroupTools.java),
[`RestGroupTemplates.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/groups/rest/RestGroupTemplates.java),
[`group-conversations.md`](../group-conversations.md); tests in `RestGroupConversationExtendedTest`,
`McpGroupToolsTest`, `RestGroupTemplatesTest`, and the constructor update in the other five
`RestGroupConversation*Test` classes.

## 🐛 fix(import): a refused merge update fails the import instead of creating a duplicate (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/import-merge-refusals`)

### What changed and why

- **A store that refuses a merge update now fails the import.** `RestImportService.updateTracked` returned `null` on any non-200 answer, and every caller then created the resource instead. Only two stores answer a refusal rather than throwing — `RestDictionaryStore.updateRegularDictionary` (a regular expression that does not compile) and `RestApiCallsStore.updateApiCalls` (batch settings over the deployment ceiling) — and for both the fallback went through `createResourceDirect`, which runs neither check: the refused document was stored as a second resource with the archive's origin id, and the workflow was pointed at the copy. `updateTracked` now throws a `WebApplicationException` carrying the store's own response (its `400` and body reach the client), or a `500` for no answer or a non-error status; the import's existing rollback undoes what the merge had already written, and a `WARN` line names the refused resource.
- **The dead fallbacks are gone.** Every other store updates through `RestVersionInfo.update`, which answers 200 or throws, so the `updated != null ? updated : create…` branches for agents, workflows, parsers, behavior, LLM, property, output, MCP calls and RAG (with its second `applyStorageRulesToNewRag`) could never run. The snippet merge no longer "falls through to create" a second snippet of the name it had just matched; a refused snippet update costs that snippet, like any snippet failure.
- **Live sync takes only a resource id as `sourceAgentId`.** `RemoteApiResourceSource.readAgent` concatenated the id into the request path, and resolving that against the source URL normalised `../` away — an id like `../../backup/export/x` read another path on the source with the caller's token. `previewSync`, `executeSync` and both batch endpoints refuse anything `RestUtilities.isValidId` rejects with a `400` (a failed entry in a batch) before a request is sent, and `readAgent` encodes the id as one path segment, as `exportAgentArchive` already did.
- The Javadocs of `RestApiCallsStore`, `RestLlmStore`, `RestMcpCallsStore` and `RedactionMarkerGuard` that described the fallback as intended are rewritten.

### Docs

[`import-export-an-agent.md`](../import-export-an-agent.md) (when a merge fails part-way) and [`agent-sync-guide.md`](../agent-sync-guide.md) (the `sourceAgentId` parameter).

### Tests

`RestImportServiceMergeSharedExtensionTest` (refused dictionary and httpcalls updates fail the merge with the store's answer, roll back, and create nothing; a non-error or missing answer is a `500`), `RestImportServiceSnippetResilienceTest` (a refused snippet update creates no duplicate and does not fail the agent), `RestImportServiceSyncCoverageTest` (a crafted `sourceAgentId` is a `400` on preview, sync and both batches, with no request sent), `RemoteApiResourceSourceTest` (the id never leaves `/agentstore/agents/`). Each failed on `main` and was mutation-checked.

**Files:** [`RestImportService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/backup/impl/RestImportService.java), [`RemoteApiResourceSource.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/backup/impl/RemoteApiResourceSource.java)

## 🐛 fix(ingestion): fetchable crawl URLs, bounded markdown prefixes, at-block hiding rules (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/ingestion-html-and-crawl`)

### What changed and why

Three ingestion defects from the whole-codebase review.

- **A link with a raw space was never fetched.** jsoup resolves `href="my doc.html"` without encoding the space.
  `CrawlUrls.canonicalize` already encoded it for the document id, but the crawler fetched the raw `abs:href`, and
  `SafeHttpPageFetcher`'s `URI.create` refused it. The page became an inconclusive error on every run, which also
  kept deletion reconciliation from ever running for the source. The new `CrawlUrls.fetchable` strips the fragment
  and encodes through the same tolerant parser (an already valid URL comes back byte-identical); `WebCrawler` uses
  it for links and for sitemap URLs, including for the `MAX_LINK_LENGTH` bound and the frontier cost. A URL that
  still does not parse is skipped instead of queued to fail.
- **Nested blockquotes and lists cost the square of their depth.** A blockquote renders into its own buffer and
  copies every line out behind `> `; a list item nested through another element (`li > div > ul`) is re-indented
  by every enclosing item. Neither budget of the walk counts those prefixes, so a 240 KB mail quoted ninety levels
  deep allocated 340 MB. `HtmlToMarkdownConverter` now caps the stack of such prefixing containers at
  `MAX_PREFIXED_NESTING = 8` per conversion (counted on the per-conversion `Walk`, so the converter stays
  stateless); deeper ones are written in place without their marker. Output up to eight levels is unchanged.
- **A hiding rule inside `@media screen` evaded the mail hidden-content pass.** `HiddenStylesheetRules` skipped
  every at-block, so `<style>@media screen{.x{display:none}}</style>` hid text from every reader and still sent it
  to the model. Blocks that hold on every screen — `@layer`, `@supports`, and `@media` with no query, `all`,
  `screen`, `only screen` or `not <other type>` — are now entered and their rules read like top-level ones.
  Entering continues the same forward scan (the block's closing brace is skipped like a stray one), so nesting
  costs no recursion and the pass stays linear. Viewport and device queries (`(max-width:600px)`,
  `prefers-color-scheme`), `print`, `@container` and the like stay skipped, because responsive mail hides one copy
  there and the other copy everywhere else.

### Design decisions

- The crawl fix encodes the URL to fetch rather than fetching the canonical id: the canonical form lowercases the
  host, drops tracking parameters and folds `index.html`, and the server should get the URL the page linked to.
- The markdown cap bounds stacking instead of rewriting the output model to stream prefixes; eight levels keep
  every real document intact.
- At-blocks are entered in place instead of collected onto a work list and re-read, which is simpler and needs
  no depth cap. A feature query that holds everywhere (`(min-width:0)`) is still skipped; the class Javadoc and
  [`security.md`](../security.md) say so.

**Files:** [`CrawlUrls.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/crawl/CrawlUrls.java),
[`WebCrawler.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/crawl/WebCrawler.java),
[`HtmlToMarkdownConverter.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/HtmlToMarkdownConverter.java),
[`HiddenStylesheetRules.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/HiddenStylesheetRules.java),
[`security.md`](../security.md), and their tests.

## 🐛 fix(managed-conversations): one claim path for the user-conversation mapping (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/managed-conversation-claims`)

### What changed and why

A managed conversation is one conversation per intent and user, held by a `UserConversation` mapping
with a unique (intent, userId) key. Two requests that both need a new conversation (none yet, or the
current one has ended) each start one and each try to store the mapping. The REST endpoints and the MCP
`chat_managed` tool handled that race differently, and both badly:

- **REST, ended conversation** (`RestAgentManagement`): the replace deleted the mapping unconditionally.
  A slower request deleted the fresh mapping a faster one had just stored, so the two clients went on
  talking to two different conversations; or its insert collided and the request failed with an opaque
  `500`.
- **REST, no mapping yet**: the loser re-read the winner, but left its own started conversation running
  with nothing pointing at it.
- **MCP `chat_managed`** (`McpConversationTools`): a lost insert fell into the generic catch and answered
  "Failed to chat via managed agent", again leaving the started conversation running. A store failure on
  the mapping read was swallowed as "no mapping", so a database hiccup started a second conversation and
  orphaned the user's real one. An agent that was not deployed yet answered a generic failure where REST
  answers `503` with `Retry-After`. And it always took the trigger's first deployment, where REST picks
  one at random.

Now there is one claim path. `IUserConversationStore.createOrAdopt` stores the candidate mapping or, on
a duplicate key, returns the mapping that won (a store error if that one is gone again by the time it is
read). Both callers use it, end the conversation they started whenever it is not the one returned
(best effort, logged on failure), and replace an ended mapping with `deleteUserConversationIfMatches`, so a
delete only removes the mapping while it still names the ended conversation. This is the pattern the
Slack, webhook and email channels already followed. MCP now lets a mapping-read failure surface, answers a
not-ready agent with `"errorCode": "UNAVAILABLE"`, and picks a random deployment like REST.

`RestAgentManagement` also takes `SecurityIdentity` and `OwnershipValidator` through its constructor.
The `{userId}` ownership check used to run only `if (ownershipValidator != null)`, a guard that turned
itself off when its dependency was missing, and the three test classes set both fields by reflection
(two of them never set the validator, so they ran with the check off). The check is now unconditional and
the tests pass both through the constructor.

**Behaviour changes:** a REST request that loses the replace race is served on the winner's conversation
instead of failing with `500`; `chat_managed` answers the same race with the winner's reply, a not-ready
agent with `UNAVAILABLE`, and spreads new conversations across a trigger's deployments. A conversation a
request started but could not claim is ended (MCP records `system:lost-create-race` as who ended it).
This holds on both stores, whether the claim fails with a checked `ResourceStoreException` (how PostgreSQL
reports a failed read or write) or with an unchecked driver exception (how MongoDB, the default backend,
reports a timeout or a write error other than a duplicate key).

**Files:** [`IUserConversationStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/triggermanagement/IUserConversationStore.java),
[`RestAgentManagement.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/RestAgentManagement.java),
[`McpConversationTools.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/mcp/McpConversationTools.java),
[`managed-agents.md`](../managed-agents.md), [`mcp-server.md`](../mcp-server.md)

**Not changed:** `EmailInboundProcessor` and `OpenAiConversationBridge` keep their own copies of the
adopt-and-end logic; moving them onto `createOrAdopt` is a follow-up.

## Manager: dialog bodies no longer touch the dialog border (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`ui/manager`) · **Branch:** `fix/manager-dialog-body-padding`

**What changed.** The "This agent is not granted its vault keys" dialog showed its
title bar with padding but its body flush against the dialog's left and right border:
the key notice box, the "Add … to the grant?" question, the "allow every agent"
checkbox and the footer buttons all sat on the edge.

**Root cause.** `AccessibleDialog` (`ui/manager/src/components/ui/accessible-dialog.tsx`)
pads only its own header (`p-5`); the content slot is rendered edge to edge on purpose,
so full-bleed bodies (the version diff editor, toolbar strips) can reach the border. Every
caller is expected to pad its own content, and 13 of the 18 call sites do (`p-5`, a few
`p-6`). Five did not:

- `ui/manager/src/components/secrets/grant-required-dialog.tsx` (the reported dialog)
- `ui/manager/src/components/feedback/ratings-table.tsx` (internal-note dialog)
- `ui/manager/src/components/quotas/team-budget-dialogs.tsx` (adjust-cap and transfer dialogs)
- `ui/manager/src/pages/user-conversations.tsx` (Create Binding dialog)

Each now wraps its body in `p-5`, matching the header. The convention is written into
the `children` doc comment of `AccessibleDialog`.

**Design decision.** Fixed at the call sites, not in the primitive: padding the content
slot by default would double-pad the 13 dialogs that already pad themselves and break the
full-bleed ones. The shared `AlertDialog` pads its whole content (`p-6`) and was fine; the
hand-rolled modals and sheets were audited and each pads its own sections. The Chat UI has
one inline confirm strip, which is padded.

## fix(manager): every workflow step opens in the Agent Studio, and a denser pipeline list (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/studio-step-editors-and-polish`)

### Why

Selecting an `eddi://ai.labs.httpcalls` step in the Agent Studio showed "Editor not available
for this extension type". That is the API-call task's *canonical* step type
(`ApiCallsTask.ID = "ai.labs.httpcalls"`; `ai.labs.apicalls` is the alias), so an agent with
thirty API-call steps had thirty uneditable stages. The same happened to steps still typed
`ai.labs.langchain` (registered by `LlmModule` as an alias of `ai.labs.llm`).

**Root cause:** the Studio resolved step → editor through its own partial type table
(`EXTENSION_TO_SLUG` in `editor-registry.tsx`), while the workflow page resolved the same step
by the store segment of its config URI (`workflow-step-links.ts`, with a third copy of the
store table) and the add-extension dialog and orphans page used a fourth
(`EXTENSION_TO_RESOURCE_SLUG`). The Studio's copy lacked both aliases.

### What changed

- **One resolution path.** `getResourceSlugForStep` in
  [`ui/manager/src/lib/api/extensions.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/api/extensions.ts) decides by
  the config URI's store segment (`apicallstore` → `apicalls`, through `RESOURCE_TYPES` and the new
  `getResourceTypeByStore`), and only falls back to the step type for a URI without that shape.
  The Studio uses it; `EXTENSION_TO_SLUG` is gone; `workflow-step-links.ts` reads the same store
  table instead of its own copy. `ai.labs.langchain` was added to the type tables.
- **Store-less steps get an informative state instead of a warning.** Templating explains what it
  does; a parser with an embedded config shows that config read-only; a step of a store-backed
  type with no URI says to attach a resource; an unknown type shows its stored config read-only.
  Each links to the workflow page (`/manage/workflowview/:id?version=N`), where steps are attached
  and inline configs are edited.
- **Pipeline list (`pipeline-railroad.tsx`).** Compact rows with the pipeline position, the
  resource's descriptor NAME (read per resource through the descriptor store, shared cache key
  with the parser editor), the id in the tooltip; consecutive steps of one type are grouped under
  one header with a count; the connector lines and per-card gaps are gone; the type label now
  comes from the shared `extensionTypes.*` labels, so it matches the workflow page.
- **Studio chrome.** Pipeline header with a step count and proper padding, larger workflow
  switcher, wider default pipeline column (288px), editor panel padded `px-6 py-5`, the shared
  `EmptyState` for "select a stage", the chat toggle as a `Button` with an `aria-label`.
- **i18n.** New `studio.inlineConfig` and `studio.stepInfo.*` keys in all 11 locales; the unused
  `studio.type.*`, `studio.noConfig` and `studio.unsupportedType` keys were removed.

### Step types and how the Studio handles them

| Step type (backend registration) | Handling |
| --- | --- |
| `ai.labs.httpcalls` (ApiCallsTask.ID), `ai.labs.apicalls` (alias) | API Calls editor (`apicallstore`) |
| `ai.labs.behavior` (RulesEvaluationTask.ID), `ai.labs.rules` (alias) | Rules editor |
| `ai.labs.llm`, `ai.labs.langchain` (alias) | LLM editor |
| `ai.labs.property` | Property Setter editor |
| `ai.labs.output` | Output editor |
| `ai.labs.mcpcalls` | MCP Calls editor |
| `ai.labs.rag` | RAG editor |
| `ai.labs.parser` with a URI / with an embedded config | Parser editor / read-only inline config + link to the workflow page |
| `ai.labs.templating` | Explanation (no config of its own) + link |
| anything else | Read-only stored config + link |

### Decisions

- The URI's store wins over the step type: it names the store the task really reads, whatever
  spelling the workflow carries. Not editing a parser's inline config in the Studio itself — that
  needs a workflow-document save, which the Studio's cascade does not do; the workflow page does.
- The "API Calls (legacy)" label for `ai.labs.httpcalls` is untouched (being decided separately);
  the pipeline list now shows it, as the workflow page already did.

### Verification

`npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run i18n:check` clean; Studio, editor-registry,
extensions, workflow, i18n and page suites pass. Mutation check: removing the store lookup from
`getResourceSlugForStep` fails the httpcalls/store tests. Checked in the browser against the MSW
mock with a 20-step pipeline.

## 🐛 fix(llm-tools): the tool-result guardrail screens the whole result before truncation; the summarizer honours a `${vars:...}` provider type (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/tool-result-screening`)

### What changed and why

**The guardrail ran on the cut text, so everything past the cut was ungoverned (LLM-3).**
`ToolLoopRunner` redacted secrets, truncated, and only then ran the tool-result
guardrail. With `truncationStrategy: paginate` the whole remainder went into
`PaginatedResponseStore` unscreened, and pages 2..N came back through
`fetch_tool_response_page`, a built-in tool. Under the documented narrowing
`directiveAppliesToSources: ["mcp", "a2a", "http"]` a built-in result is not
screened for directives, so an instruction an MCP server placed after the first
page reached the model unredacted, in an envelope that said `builtin`. The
`summarize` strategy likewise sent the unscreened text to its summarizer model.
The default configuration (an empty source list means every source) was not
affected.

`ToolResultGuardrail.inspect` is split in two. `screen` runs the directive rule
on the whole result and applies `warn`/`redact`/`block`; `mark` adds the
provenance envelope. The runner now screens first, truncates the screened text,
and marks what is left, so stored pages and summarizer input are governed text
under the tool's real source. A summary is screened once more, because it is new
text a model wrote from third-party content. Both methods keep the
degrade-to-allow behaviour.

Behaviour changes: `block` now also fires on a directive in the part a
`headTail` or `truncate` cut drops. The `tool_result` trace entry shows the
screened text (without the envelope). `eddi_guardrail_toolresult_count_total`
counts a `summarize` summary as a second screening.

**The summarizer ignored global variables in the provider type (LLM-11).**
`ToolResponseTruncator` passed `task.getType()` unresolved to
`ChatModelRegistry.getOrCreate`, so a task typed `${vars:provider}` failed to
build a summarizer and `summarize` silently became `truncate`. The type is now
resolved through `GlobalVariableResolver`, as `LlmTask` resolves it. Parameters
need no such step: the registry resolves `${vars:...}` and vault references in
them itself, while a Qute expression in a parameter stays unrendered (now noted in
the class Javadoc). `PaginatedResponseStore` moved into the same constructor, which
removed a null-fallback branch for a field CDI always fills and the reflection
the tests used to set it.

### Design decisions

- **Screen before truncation and mark after, rather than screening each page as
  it is fetched.** Per-page screening would need the original source carried
  through the page store and would still miss a directive split across a page
  boundary. Screening once, whole, covers every strategy at once.
- **Re-screen only the summary.** The cut strategies only remove text from an
  already-screened result, so scanning their output again would cost a second
  regex pass and a second metric count on the default path for nothing.
- **Resolve the type in the truncator**, the one place that builds the summarizer
  model, rather than in the runner.

**Files:** [`ToolResultGuardrail.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/guardrails/ToolResultGuardrail.java), [`ToolLoopRunner.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/ToolLoopRunner.java), [`ToolResponseTruncator.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/ToolResponseTruncator.java), tests in `ToolLoopRunnerGuardrailWiringTest`, `ToolResultGuardrailTest`, `ToolResponseTruncatorTest`, `ToolResponseTruncatorExtendedTest`, `HelperModelCallRetryTest`; docs [`langchain.md`](../langchain.md), [`mcp-client.md`](../mcp-client.md), [`metrics.md`](../metrics.md).

## 🐛 fix(turns): queued-turn guard on the one-read path, busy skips and inert abandoned outcomes (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/turn-runner-guards`)

### What changed and why

Four holes in how a queued turn starts and how an abandoned one ends, all in `ConversationStepRunner`
plus one in the SSE stream.

- **A conversation deleted while its turn was queued (RT-1).** Both stores answer a missing document
  with no state and no revision. The queued-say guard skipped only `AWAITING_HUMAN`, `IN_PROGRESS` and
  `ENDED`, so a turn queued during a delete or a GDPR erasure ran on its request-time snapshot: model and
  tool calls, and `storePropertiesPermanently` wrote the erased user's long-term properties back after
  the erasure's memory sweep. Only the final persist failed. Such a turn is now skipped and reported as
  `ENDED`: REST answers `410`, a stream ends with `done`, and the clients already start a new conversation
  on `ENDED`.
- **A store failure at that guard (RT-2).** The state/revision read ran outside any `try`. A pool timeout
  there escaped the task, the coordinator dead-lettered the turn, and its caller never heard back (REST
  waited for its `408`, a stream got no terminal event). The same held for a runtime that refused the
  body (`RejectedExecutionException`). Both now answer **busy** (`409` + `Retry-After`, `done` with
  `IN_PROGRESS` on a stream; the input was not consumed), the contract the fence branch already used for a
  store failure. The three busy paths share one `skipAsBusy`.
- **The late outcome of a turn the watchdog abandoned (RT-5).** After a watchdog timeout the coordinator
  starts the next turn while a body that swallowed its interrupt keeps running. When it finally ended, its
  callbacks settled it as a cancelled turn, keyed only by conversation id: on one node that stamped
  `EXECUTION_INTERRUPTED` over the `READY` the next turn had committed (and made that turn's pause commit
  miss); in cluster mode its expired lease put a lease-lost record in the map the next turn drains, so the
  next turn was dead-lettered as lease-lost after it had answered `200`. Once the dispatcher has given up,
  the outcome now only refreshes the state cache. The same holds for a persist the completion callback
  started just before the watchdog gave up and that failed afterwards: a fence or revision-guard refusal is
  no longer recorded for the next turn to drain (which dead-lettered that turn), and a store failure no
  longer stamps `ERROR` over the next turn's state. The comments that said a node runs at most one turn of a
  conversation at a time were wrong and are corrected.
- **A stream whose turn hung past the watchdog (RT-2).** REST has its `408`; a stream had nothing and was
  kept open by the keep-alive for as long as the body hung. Once the turn has written its first event, a
  stream that then carries none of its events for `systemRuntime.agentTimeoutInSeconds` plus one
  keep-alive interval is ended with an `error` event, code `turn_timeout`. A live turn cannot reach that
  (the watchdog bounds it), a queued one has written nothing yet, and with the keep-alive off the backstop
  is off too.

`ConversationServiceQueuedTurnTest` now stubs `getStateAndRevision`, the path both production stores
take; before, every queued-turn test ran on the legacy two-read fallback. One test keeps the fallback
covered.

**Files:** [`ConversationStepRunner.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/ConversationStepRunner.java),
[`RestAgentEngineStreaming.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/RestAgentEngineStreaming.java),
[`configuration-reference.md`](../configuration-reference.md), [`hitl.md`](../hitl.md) (the resume stream's
`turn_timeout`), [`rest-api-reference.md`](../rest-api-reference.md) (the stream's `turn_timeout` code); tests in `ConversationServiceQueuedTurnTest`,
`ConversationStepRunnerLeaseTest`, `ConversationStepRunnerAbandonedTurnTest` (new) and
`RestAgentEngineStreamingKeepAliveTest`.

## 🐛 fix(llm-tools): per-turn tool budgets live on the conversation step, forgetFact included (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/turn-scoped-tool-budgets`)

### What changed and why

Two LLM-tool budgets were counted in a field of the tool object: `maxWritesPerTurn` in `UserMemoryTool`
and `maxDelegationsPerTask` in `ConverseWithAgentTool`. A turn builds its tools more than once — each
step of an agent-mode model cascade runs its own tool assembly, and so does the continuation of a turn
resumed after a HITL tool approval — and every assembly starts a new tool object at zero. A three-step
escalating cascade could therefore write three times `maxWritesPerTurn`, and a resumed turn got a fresh
budget, while [`user-memory.md`](../user-memory.md) promised a limit "per conversation turn".

The new `TurnCounter` keeps the count on the conversation's current step instead, where
`hitl:tool_pause_count` already lives for the same reason. `ContextualToolsProvider` hands the memory
tool `tools:memory_writes`, and `DynamicAgentToolsProvider` hands the delegation tool
`tools:delegations:<taskId>` (`default` for a task without an id), so the delegation cap stays per LLM
task as documented, now across every assembly of that task in the turn. The next turn's step starts at
zero again. Two consequences follow from counting on the step: the LLM tasks of one turn share
`maxWritesPerTurn` (it was per task execution), and a rerun of the turn, which re-executes the same step,
starts from what its first run spent — the writes and delegations that run made are not undone either.
Neither key has a public prefix, so the counts stay out of the simple client snapshot; `returnDetailed=true` shows them as plain integers, like `hitl:tool_pause_count`. The key is read by exact
match: the step's `getLatestData` is a prefix lookup and would let `tools:delegations:a`
read the count of `tools:delegations:ab`. A tool built without a conversation (direct construction)
still counts on its own instance.

`forgetFact` was not covered by any budget, so one injected "forget everything about the user" could
delete every memory the agent owns in a single turn. A successful deletion now counts against
`maxWritesPerTurn` like a stored fact, and is refused with the same "Maximum writes per turn" message
once the budget is spent. A key that matches nothing and a refused deletion do not count. The refusal
text now says "saved or forgotten", since it can answer either tool.

**Files:** [`TurnCounter.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/tools/TurnCounter.java),
[`UserMemoryTool.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/tools/UserMemoryTool.java),
[`ConverseWithAgentTool.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/tools/ConverseWithAgentTool.java),
[`ContextualToolsProvider.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/ContextualToolsProvider.java),
[`DynamicAgentToolsProvider.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/DynamicAgentToolsProvider.java),
[`user-memory.md`](../user-memory.md)

## fix(nlp): a chat message with stray parentheses no longer fails the turn (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/user-input-expression-depth`)

A production deployment on 6.6.0 failed three turns in five minutes when users pasted long text containing list
markers and unbalanced parentheses (`a)`, `(It`, `3))`). The log showed `Error while parsing Expression` repeated, then
`ExpressionLimitExceededException: Expressions nest 33 levels deep; at most 32 are accepted`, and the user received
the generic fallback answer.

**Root cause:** every token no dictionary knew became `unknown(<token>)` (`Unknown`), and `InputParserTask` stores the
expressions of a turn as one string that rule matching, the property setter and the append merge parse again. The
token text went into that string verbatim, so each `(` in the message became an opening bracket in expression syntax.
Opening brackets from different tokens add up across the whole message; past 32 the depth bound added in 6.6 refused
the string and the exception failed the lifecycle. Before the bound the same input produced silently wrong nesting.
`main` still had it.

**Fix:** `Unknown` now builds `unknown(<token>)` from the token without `(`, `)` and `,`, the three characters the
notation gives meaning to; a token that is only such characters becomes a bare `unknown`. The word itself (`getValue()`,
match details, the conversation input) is untouched, and the other dictionaries that build expressions from runtime
text only accept digits, e-mail addresses or epoch times, which cannot carry those characters. The malformed-expression
log in `ExpressionProvider` is a WARN instead of an ERROR.

**Decision:** `eddi.expressions.max-depth` and `eddi.expressions.max-length` are unchanged and still refuse
configuration-authored and client-supplied (`context` type `expressions`) nesting; user chat text is data and is
sanitised where it becomes an expression, not by loosening the guard.

**Tests:** `UserInputExpressionRoundTripTest` runs prose with many unbalanced parentheses through the parser, the
stored expression string and the re-parse (failed with the 33-level error before the fix), checks that dictionary words
still match, and that authored deep nesting is still refused.

## 🛠️ fix(manager): Active Tools in the Workforce agent panel shows what the LLM really gets (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/workforce-active-tools-display`)

### Why

The Workforce chat's Agent Details panel listed an LLM task's `tools` as raw
`eddi://ai.labs.apicalls/apicallstore/apicalls/<id>?version=1` URIs, one badge each. The badges
overflowed the 320 px panel (horizontal scrollbar), an agent with sixteen tool sets produced a
wall of near-identical badges, and nothing said which tool set was which.

Worse, it was the wrong list. The backend never resolves `task.tools`
(`LlmConfiguration.Task#tools`: "a non-empty list only switches the task into agent mode").
HTTP and MCP tools are discovered from the `eddi://ai.labs.httpcalls` / `eddi://ai.labs.mcpcalls`
steps of **every** workflow of the agent (`HttpCallToolsProvider`, `McpToolsProvider`,
`WorkflowTraversal.discoverConfigs`), gated by `enableHttpCallTools` / `enableMcpCallTools`,
which default to **on**. The panel showed the HTTP/MCP chips only when those flags were
explicitly `true`, and showed a built-in whitelist even when `enableBuiltInTools` was off, in
which case the backend ignores it.

### What changed

- `ui/manager/src/lib/agent-tool-refs.ts` (new): `collectWorkflowToolRefs` reads tool sets from
  the agent's workflows the way the backend does. It matches the step type exactly (a step typed
  with the `eddi://ai.labs.apicalls` alias is not offered as a tool by the backend, so it is not
  listed), respects the two switches with default-on semantics, and deduplicates.
- `ui/manager/src/components/workforce/active-tools-list.tsx` (new): the Active Tools section.
  - Each tool set is shown by its **descriptor name** (one cached `getDescriptor` read, short-id
    fallback), with an HTTP/MCP icon, its version, the full URI in the tooltip and a link to its
    editor.
  - The first four are shown; the rest sit behind "Show N more".
  - Built-in tools appear only when `enableBuiltInTools` is on: the whitelist as chips, or one
    "All built-in tools" chip when there is no whitelist.
  - There is a Memory chip and a total count.
  - A loading state shows while the workflows load.
- `agent-details-panel.tsx`:
  - It fetches every workflow of the agent, sharing the existing query key so the first workflow
    comes from cache.
  - It uses the new component and no longer repeats capabilities in the tool list.
  - It clips horizontal overflow, and the model badge truncates.
- Six new `Workforce.agentEditor.tools*` keys in all 11 locales.

### Tests

`active-tools-list.test.tsx` has 9 tests:

- collection across workflows, with dedupe;
- the `apicalls` alias is ignored;
- the per-category switches;
- `task.tools` is not listed;
- name resolution, link and collapse;
- the id fallback on a 404 descriptor;
- built-ins gated by `enableBuiltInTools`;
- the loading and empty states.

Mutation check: when the HTTP switch is flipped from default-on to default-off, 2 tests fail.

### Follow-ups found on the way (not in this PR)

- Pipeline labels: `extensions.ts` labels the canonical `eddi://ai.labs.httpcalls` step type
  "API Calls (legacy)" and the alias "API Calls". It is the other way round: `ApiCallsTask.ID`
  is `ai.labs.httpcalls`, and `V6RenameMigration` deliberately keeps that step type.
- Backend: a workflow step typed `eddi://ai.labs.apicalls` runs, but its calls are never offered
  to the LLM as tools, because `WorkflowTraversal` matches the type string exactly.
  `docs/architecture.md` shows that alias in an example.

## 🔒 fix(workspaces): legacy resources grant at most EDIT; settings load once and revalidate (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/workspace-legacy-access`)

### What changed and why

- **A resource that predates ownership no longer makes every caller its owner.**
  `DescriptorAccess.effectiveLevel` gave `OWN` to any caller for an unowned descriptor
  under `legacy-visibility=shared`. `OWN` gates delete, share, revoke, visibility and
  move, so any user could publish a colleague's pre-upgrade agent to anonymous callers,
  or move it into `user:<them>` — after which it was owned, and everyone else lost
  access. With enforcement off, the same answer let any user read the grant list of a
  legacy resource. The legacy contribution is now `EDIT`: everyone can still read,
  use, edit and deploy pre-existing resources, so an upgrade hides nothing, but the
  `OWN` acts are admin-only until an owner is assigned with
  `PUT /descriptorstore/descriptors/{id}/shares/owner`. Explicit grants on a legacy
  resource still count in full. It is the rule a resource with no descriptor at all
  already followed — an absent record must not grant authority — though that case,
  in `ResourceAccessGuard.requireLegacyFallback`, stops lower still, at read and use. See
  [Resources that predate ownership](../workspaces.md#resources-that-predate-ownership).
- **The workspace settings cache no longer makes access checks wait for the store.**
  `WorkspaceSettings` re-read the stored settings under a lock when its 5-second cache
  expired, so every request whose access check asked `admitsLegacy()` queued behind a
  database read with no timeout — at every expiry, and for the whole of an outage. It is
  now stale-while-revalidate: one caller re-reads, everyone else is served the last
  values. Each read is numbered when it starts and each adopted write when it lands, and
  the higher number is what stays served, so neither a write adopted meanwhile nor the
  settings page's forced re-read is undone by an older read that answers later. Only the
  very first read, with nothing to serve, is still waited for. No executor is involved.
- **A corrupt stored `legacy-visibility` is validated once, at load.** It used to be
  re-validated, and logged at `WARN`, on every access check. It is now treated as unset
  when read, logged once per distinct bad value, and `GET /workspaces/settings` reports
  it as `DEFAULT` — the default that actually applies.
- **`PUT /workspaces/settings` stamps `updatedAt` to the millisecond.** Mongo keeps
  milliseconds and Postgres microseconds, so the value this instance adopted differed
  from what a replica read back.
- **New `WorkspaceSettingsStoreParityTest`** runs the Mongo and Postgres settings stores
  through the same cases: unknown tenant, full round trip, unset fields read back as
  null, writing null clears a stored value (how `PUT` unsets a setting), tenant
  isolation, timestamp precision. Neither store had a test before.

### Behaviour change for administrators of upgraded deployments

With `eddi.workspaces.enabled=true`, a non-admin can no longer delete, share, publish,
move or read the grants of a resource created before ownership was recorded; the
Manager stops offering those actions on it (it reports `callerLevel: EDIT`). Assign an
owner to each such resource you want someone other than an administrator to manage.
Nothing changes for administrators, or while enforcement is off apart from the grant
list.

**Files:** [`DescriptorAccess.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/security/spaces/DescriptorAccess.java),
[`WorkspaceSettings.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/security/spaces/WorkspaceSettings.java),
[`RestWorkspaces.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/security/spaces/rest/RestWorkspaces.java),
[`workspaces.md`](../workspaces.md); tests in `DescriptorAccessTest`, `ResourceAccessGuardTest`,
`RuntimeWorkspaceSettingsTest` and the new `WorkspaceSettingsStoreParityTest`.

## 🐛 fix(compat): no false TEMPLATE_DOES_NOT_PARSE for a prompt that mentions a vault reference (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/compat-template-parse-vault-prose`)

### What changed and why

Every Platform Operator deployment logged `[TEMPLATE_DOES_NOT_PARSE] llm task '…'.parameters.systemMessage: … empty
expression found {vault:…}, so every render of it fails`, while the agent answered correctly. The warning was a false
positive: at runtime `LlmTask.runTemplateEngineOnParams` first wraps `{vault:…}`, `{vars:…}`, `{connection:…}` and
`{caller:…}` mentions in Qute raw sections (`escapeConfigReferenceMentions`), so a prompt that only documents the syntax
renders fine. The deploy-time lint parsed the unescaped text.

- [`CompatibilityRules.scanLlmTaskTemplates`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/compat/CompatibilityRules.java)
  applies the same escaping before parsing, and only to the task's own `parameters` — the strings the runtime escapes.
  Cascade step parameters, API-call, property and output templates are rendered without it and still warn.
  `AgentCompatibilityLint` uses it for LLM tasks; `LlmTask.escapeConfigReferenceMentions` is now public for this.
- A genuinely broken template next to a mention still warns. No Manager change, so no operator revision bump.

## 📝 docs(connections): Google Workspace recipe — Gmail and Drive as per-user LLM tools (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`docs/google-workspace-tools-recipe`)

### What changed and why

The Google Workspace plan's conversation-tools slice. It is configuration apart from one engine
addition, the `encoder:mimeHeader` template function that `createDraft` uses for the subject (see
below), so the recipe needs a build that includes it. Per-user Google access was the motivating case for connections, but no worked config
showed it end to end, and the prerequisites (verified identity, allowlisted credential endpoints,
Google's publishing-status rules) were scattered or missing.

- **Recipe** — [`docs/agent-configs/google-workspace-tools/`](../agent-configs/google-workspace-tools/),
  an importable agent in the ZIP layout: the `google-workspace` connection
  (`OAUTH2_AUTHORIZATION_CODE`, `PER_USER`, scopes `gmail.readonly` + `gmail.compose` +
  `drive.readonly`), a Gmail httpcalls config (`searchMail`, `readThread`, `createDraft`), a Drive one
  (`searchDrive`, `readDriveFile`), an LLM task with httpcall tools on and built-in tools off, and the
  setup wizard's catch-all rule. Every tool has an `argumentSchema` with `unknownFields: reject` and
  authenticates with `"Authorization": "${connection:google-workspace}"`.
- **[`google-workspace.md`](../google-workspace.md)** (new, in `SUMMARY.md` under Connections) —
  prerequisites (OIDC-verified users and which entry points are `SELF_ASSERTED`; connection settings
  with both Google origins on the credential-endpoint allowlist; the OAuth client; Linked accounts,
  including that only an admin can *start* a link from that page), Google's publishing-status table,
  installation, and the security notes below.
- **[`email-integration.md`](../email-integration.md#gmail-and-google-workspace)** — the Gmail note
  expanded: why an inbound Gmail integration is refused at save (authorization-code grants are
  `PER_USER`, a poll has no verified principal — `ConnectionResolver.requireMailLoginTarget`), that app
  passwords over `BASIC` are not blocked but untested, and what is planned (admin-linked `SERVICE`
  grant, domain-wide delegation).
- **`StrictBoundaryShippedConfigsTest`** now maps `.connection.json` to `ConnectionConfiguration`, so
  the recipe's connection — and any future fixture under `connections/` — is parsed strictly instead
  of being counted as skipped.
- Cross-links from [`connections.md`](../connections.md#see-also) and
  [`agent-config-authoring.md`](../agent-config-authoring.md#reference-implementation).

### Design decisions

- **No send tool; drafts only.** A model that reads mail and can send is an exfiltration channel for
  prompt injection in mail bodies. The page says how to gate a send tool behind
  `hitlConfig.toolApprovals`, and that `gmail.compose` already permits sending — the "no send" is the
  tool set's, not the token's.
- **CR/LF in `to`/`subject` is neutralised in the template, not rejected by the schema.** The argument
  schema check deliberately drops `pattern` (backtracking risk on model text), so a pattern forbidding
  `\r\n` would accept everything. Instead `to` has CR and LF removed with `.replace(…)` and `subject`
  goes through the new `encoder:mimeHeader`; the body is `base64Mime` after the header block.
- **`encoder:mimeHeader` (new, `EddiTemplateExtensions`).** The first version wrapped the whole subject
  in one `=?UTF-8?B?…?=` word, which breaks RFC 2047's 75-character word limit from 46 UTF-8 bytes up (24
  `é`); the review caught it. The function always B-encodes, ASCII included, so it keeps the CR/LF
  guarantee; Jakarta Mail's `MimeUtility.encodeText` was rejected because it leaves all-ASCII text,
  CR and LF included, unencoded. Words carry at most 39 bytes (64 characters framed, so `Subject: `
  plus the first word fits a 76-character line), split on code points and joined with CRLF SP.
  `EncoderExtensionsTest` round-trips them through `MimeUtility.decodeText`.
- **`StrictBoundaryShippedConfigsTest.shippedConnectionsImport`.** The strict sweep swallows malformed
  JSON and wrong value types, and the importer silently skips a connection it cannot read, leaving the
  agent's `${connection:…}` reference unresolved. Every shipped `*.connection.json` is now read as
  `RestImportService` reads it and passed through `ConnectionConfiguration.validate()`. Verified against qute-core 3.40.1.1: a raw CR/LF inside a Qute string literal is kept,
  and nested namespace calls work as `str:concat` arguments.
- **`readThread` returns Gmail's base64url body parts as-is** — templating has no decoder; documented
  as a limitation rather than worked around.

### What's next

A real `pattern` (or a narrower "no control characters" keyword) for tool arguments would let such
fields be refused rather than rewritten. Admin-linked `SERVICE` grants and domain-wide delegation for
Gmail as an inbound channel are later slices of the plan.

---

## ✨ feat(outbound): a Vert.x engine for OutboundHttp, with a pre-send connected-peer check (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/outbound-vertx-engine`)

Phase 4 of [`planning/http-client-consolidation-plan.md`](../../planning/http-client-consolidation-plan.md), which this branch also adds — it was referenced by Phases 1 and 2 but never merged.

### What changed and why

On the JDK engine the facade can only re-check a target **after** the exchange, so a DNS-rebinding request has already been sent blind. Moving `httpcalls` (Phase 5) onto that engine would lose the pre-send check its own Vert.x client has today. This adds the engine Phase 5 needs.

- **`VertxOutboundEngine`** (`engine/httpclient/outbound`) — the core Vert.x `HttpClient` of the Quarkus `Vertx`, one client per profile (TLS and cleartext separately for `protocol=auto`). No redirects, no decompression, no Vert.x tracing (the facade writes and strips trace headers). The request target is built from the validated `URI`'s parts; a `URI` with no host is refused rather than re-parsed (security review H2).
- **`OutboundEngine.Hop.peerCheck`** — run on the address the connection reached, after it is assigned and before a byte is written. It runs on every request, so a pooled connection is judged each time it is handed out. A refused connection is closed, not just reset: resetting a request with nothing written yet hands an HTTP/1 connection back to the Vert.x pool, still open to the refused address until the keep-alive timeout, and the close is what evicts it. The JDK engine ignores it; the post-exchange recheck stays on both. Skipped through a forward proxy, whose peer is the proxy.
- **`ConnectedPeers`** — the address rules, once: `STRICT` refuses loopback/private/link-local/metadata peers, `METADATA_ONLY` metadata/link-local ones, `HOPS_ONLY` only on redirect hops. `HttpClientModule` parses peers through it.
- **`VertxBodyStream`** — the event-loop-to-blocking bridge: bounded queue (pause at 256 KiB, resume below 64 KiB), ordered end/failure, reset on early close, and a close that wakes a blocked reader.
- **Selection** — `eddi.http.outbound.engine` now takes `jdk` or `vertx`; new per-profile `eddi.http.outbound.<profile>.engine` and `…max-connections-per-host` (fails startup on a `jdk` profile). `apicalls` defaults to `vertx` — no caller sends through it until Phase 5, so nothing changes at runtime today. `ProfileRoutedEngine` dispatches; the Vert.x engine is built only when a profile uses it.
- **Metrics** — `eddi.http.outbound.requests` and `eddi.http.outbound.duration` gain an `engine` tag.
- **Docs** — `configuration-reference.md` (new keys), `security.md` (DNS rebinding table), `application.properties` comments.

### Tests

`VertxOutboundHttpServerTest` reruns the whole `OutboundHttpServerTest` suite on Vert.x; `VertxOutboundEngineTest` (loopback: refused peer receives zero bytes, host differential, cap mid-stream, deadline, proxy, pool-limit queuing); `VertxOutboundEnginePeerRefusalTest` (mocks: a refused connection is closed before the reset and nothing is sent, a second request on the same pooled connection is checked and refused again, an allowed peer's connection stays open); `VertxOutboundEnginePoolIT` (loopback: two refused requests to one origin, pool limit 1, open two connections, each closed by the client with zero bytes received); `VertxBodyStreamTest`; `OutboundEngineSelectionTest` (settings, and which peer check each hop carries).

### Design decisions

- The peer check is a callback on the hop, so the rules sit in the facade and an engine only says where it connected.
- A request with its own `hopValidator` gets no peer check on redirect hops: its validator decides what a hop may reach.
- Cleartext `auto` is HTTP/1.1 on Vert.x — neither JDK-style `h2c` upgrade nor prior-knowledge HTTP/2.

### What's next

Phase 5 (`httpcalls` onto the facade). It needs the previous hop's connected class and the allow-list in the peer check (plan §5 item 5), and pool gauges for Vert.x profiles.

---

## ✨ feat(hitl): approval bound to the exact plan — `planHash` (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/hitl-plan-hash`)

### What changed and why

A HITL approval approved "this pause", but nothing tied it to the exact arguments or actions that then ran. Every pause now gets a **plan hash** when it is committed — inspect → plan hash → apply only with that hash:

- **What is hashed** ([`HitlPlanHash`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/hitl/HitlPlanHash.java)): SHA-256 over a canonical JSON document, reported as `v1:` + 64 hex. A tool pause covers the conversation, agent and version, the batch's `pauseEpoch`, a per-pause secret nonce, and every pending call in order (`callId`, `toolName`, `source`, `argsTruncated`, canonicalized raw arguments, request fingerprint when pinned). A rule (turn-level) pause covers the bookmark (pause id, workflow, task index) and the paused step's actions without `PAUSE_CONVERSATION`. A group pause covers the paused phase's transcript, the tasks awaiting approval and the synthesized answer.
- **Canonicalization**: keys sorted by UTF-16 code unit, arrays in order, numbers by value (`1` = `1.0` = `1e0`), strings as parsed (escapes decoded, no Unicode normalization); arguments that are not exactly one JSON document (duplicate keys, anything after the first value) are hashed as their exact text. The existing `JacksonCanonicalizer` was not reused because it keeps Jackson's number formatting (`1` ≠ `1.0`).
- **Stored with the pause**: `hitlPlanHash` on the conversation (cleared with the bookmark) and `planHash` + `planNonce` on the pending tool batch. Computed in `ConversationHitlService#populateHitlTimeoutBookmark`, i.e. after the turn's secret scrub rewrote the batch and before the store, so it covers exactly what is persisted. The nonce is never served (approver projection nulls it, names-only projection never copies it, the Mongo and PostgreSQL tail reads leave it on the server).
- **Checks** ([`HitlPlanBinding`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/hitl/HitlPlanBinding.java), one rule for both surfaces): a decision whose `planHash` is not the pause's → `409` before the CAS and again on the snapshot the resume runs; the resume re-derives the hash from the loaded plan and refuses (`409`, `WARN hitl.plan.changed`, pause restored) when it no longer matches; `ToolLoopResumer` re-derives it once more immediately before an approved call runs (`NOT_EXECUTED`, "the plan changed after it was approved").
- **Compatibility**: a decision without `planHash` is accepted and logged (`hitl.plan.not_presented`) unless strict binding is on — `hitlConfig.requirePlanHash` (agent or group) or `eddi.hitl.plan-hash.strict=true` — then it is a `400` and the pause is not consumed. `system:*` decisions are exempt. Pauses committed before this change have no hash and are decided as before, also under strict binding (`hitl.plan.legacy`).
- **A hash that cannot be computed fails closed under strict binding**: `HitlPlanHash.commit` never throws, so a hashing bug cannot stop pauses from committing, but the pause it could not hash stores the marker `"unavailable"` (conversation `hitlPlanHash` and batch `planHash`; it persists through both stores like any hash) instead of null. A null would have made it indistinguishable from a legacy pause, which strict binding accepts unbound. `HitlPlanBinding.check` refuses a human decision on a marked pause under strict binding (`409`, the pause is restored as for a mismatch). Without strict binding it accepts the decision, logs `WARN hitl.plan.unavailable` and records it as `PLAN_HASH_UNAVAILABLE`. `approval-status` (REST, MCP) and Slack cards serve `""` for the marker, and it never matches. Group plans are derived at decision time, not stored, so a failed derivation already fails the approve.
- **Surfaces**: REST `approval-status` (`planHash`) and the resume/approve bodies (`HitlDecision.planHash`); MCP `get_approval_status` / `get_group_approval_status` report it, `resume_conversation` / `approve_group_phase` take an optional `planHash` (`PLAN_CHANGED`, `PLAN_HASH_REQUIRED`); Slack buttons carry it as a fourth value field and the edit/reject modals keep it in their metadata, and a card whose plan is no longer the pending one is replaced by a "Not applied — the request has changed" notice without buttons; the Manager's `lib/hitl-pause-binding.ts` sends the shown plan from every approval surface, and the operator card sends it too.
- **Audit**: `hitl.approval` entries carry `planHash` and `planBinding` (`MATCHED` / `NOT_PRESENTED` / `SYSTEM` / `LEGACY_UNHASHED` / `PLAN_HASH_UNAVAILABLE`); every task a resume runs carries `input.hitlPlanHash`; `hitl_approved` trace entries carry `planHash`.
- **Platform Operator**: system prompt documents plan binding and strict mode; revision 60 → 61. It also names the `PLAN_HASH_UNAVAILABLE` outcome and what a pause whose hash could not be computed does under strict and non-strict binding.

**Docs:** [hitl.md → Plan Binding](../hitl.md#plan-binding-planhash), [hitl-review-and-audit.md](../hitl-review-and-audit.md), [hitl-approval-context.md](../hitl-approval-context.md), [audit-ledger.md](../audit-ledger.md), [mcp-server.md](../mcp-server.md), [configuration-reference.md](../configuration-reference.md), [cryptography.md](../cryptography.md).

### Design decisions

- **Strict mode at two levels** (agent/group `hitlConfig.requirePlanHash` and deployment `eddi.hitl.plan-hash.strict`), either one enabling it: a designer protects high-stakes agents without breaking other clients; an operator turns it on everywhere once all clients send the hash. A failed config read counts as strict (fail closed).
- **Group plans are derived, not stored**: an `AWAITING_APPROVAL` discussion cannot change except through the approval, so the read-time hash is the stored state's hash.
- **Amended arguments are not re-hashed**: the hash binds what the reviewer was shown; the amendment is the reviewer's own recorded change.
- **The tool-call plan omits the pause id** (it uses the batch's `pauseEpoch` instead) so the tool loop can re-derive it after the bookmark is cleared.

### What's next

- Strict binding could become the default once every first-party client has shipped a release that sends the hash.

## feat(rag): retrieval benchmark — golden queries, recall@k, MRR, baseline diff (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/rag-retrieval-eval`)

### Why

There was no way to tell whether a RAG change — chunking, embedding model, `minScore` — made
retrieval better or worse. `POST /ragstore/rags/{id}/search` shows one query at a time, and
comparing two configurations meant eyeballing result lists.

### What changed

- **`POST /ragstore/rags/{id}/evaluate?version=N`** (`IRestRagIngestion`, `RestRagIngestion`):
  takes golden queries `[{id?, query, expected[], k?}]`, runs each through the **same**
  `KnowledgeBaseInspector.search` call `/search` uses (no second retriever), and returns per query
  `firstHitRank`, `hit` (hit@k), `reciprocalRank`, `recall` (recall@k), `found`, `missing` and the
  ranked identifiers retrieved, plus `summary` (`hitRate`, `recall`, `mrr`). With a `baseline` (an
  earlier response's `queries`) it adds a `comparison`: per-query and aggregate deltas, regressions
  flagged. EDIT, like `/search`.
- **`RetrievalMetrics`** (`modules/rag/evaluation`): the metric math as a pure class, unit-tested on
  its own.
- **`eddi.rag.evaluation.max-queries`** (default `100`): queries per request; more is a 400 naming
  the setting. Every validation (empty query, no expected documents, `k` outside 1–50, `minScore`
  outside 0–1, duplicate query keys) runs before the first embedding call.
- Docs: [rag.md → Evaluating retrieval](../rag.md#evaluating-retrieval), the endpoint table, and
  [configuration-reference.md](../configuration-reference.md). OpenAPI snapshot for the Manager's
  contract test gains the operation.

### Design decisions

- **Hit = exact, case-sensitive equality** of an expected identifier with the value of one of the
  `matchKeys` in a chunk's metadata. Default keys `source` (a direct ingest's `documentName`),
  `documentId` and `url` (an ingestion source's document), so both ingestion paths work without
  configuration; `matchKeys` overrides them (e.g. `title` for an upload's file name).
- **recall@k is per-document** (distinct expected documents found / expected, averaged), and hit
  rate is returned beside it — they differ whenever a query expects more than one document.
  Chunks of one document count once.
- **Reciprocal rank is cut at k** (0 without a hit in the top k), so `mrr` is MRR@k.
- **A query with nothing expected is refused** (400) at the endpoint; the math class treats it as
  unscorable (null recall, left out of every average) rather than as a perfect or failed answer.
- **Baseline pairing by `id`, else query text**; duplicate keys on either side are a 400. Aggregate
  deltas are over the queries both runs contain; the rest are listed (`onlyInCurrent`,
  `onlyInBaseline`). A query **regressed** if its reciprocal rank *or* recall fell; `kChanged`
  marks a different cut-off.
- **A knowledge base `maxResults` above 50 is clamped** to the search ceiling for the default k,
  while an explicit `k` above 50 is refused — the benchmark never reports a k it did not look at.
- **One failure fails the run** (502 with `queryIndex`), no partial results: a benchmark missing
  queries compares badly with its baseline.

### Not done / next

- No MCP tool (there is no RAG tool family yet; adding one means a new tool class, roles and the
  tool-count docs) and no Manager UI. The Platform Operator is not given the endpoint, matching
  `/search`, which it also does not hold.

---

## ♻️ refactor(ingestion): provider-neutral source connector contract (F1) (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`refactor/ingestion-source-connector-contract`)

### What changed and why

This is the foundation of the ingestion source types plan (`planning/rag-ingestion-source-types-plan.md` §3.1, §3.2, §3.5).
`IngestionPipeline` was hard-wired to the web crawler and the uploaded-file store. It now dispatches
every run through a `SourceConnector` chosen by `IngestionSource.type`, so that provider-specific Java
lives only in connectors. **There is no behaviour change**: the existing ingestion tests pass without
modification.

- **The contract** (new package `modules/ingestion/connector`):
  - `SourceConnector`: `discover(DiscoveryContext) → DiscoveryResult` lists from a cursor and streams
    `WorkItem`s to `context.accept`. `fetch(WorkItem, SourceContext) → SourceDocument` reads one
    document. `listPartitions` and `verifyAccess` default to one implicit, always-accessible partition.
  - `WorkItem` carries `action` `UPSERT`/`UNCHANGED`/`DELETE`, a version token, hints, and an
    optional prefetched document.
  - `SourceDocument` has `Content` `HtmlPage`/`FileBytes`/`Text`, a version token, HTTP validators
    and metadata.
  - `SourceMetadataKeys` defines the standard vocabulary and reserves `acl.principals`, which is
    schema only.
  - `Coverage` is `FULL_LISTING`/`FULL_TRAVERSAL`/`INCREMENTAL`/`PARTIAL`. `StopReason` uses the same
    names as `WebCrawler.StopReason`.
  - `SourceError` is a sealed type: `Transient`, `Throttled`, `Auth`, `NotFound`, `Permanent`,
    `Rejected` and `CursorInvalid`, plus `isDefinitive()`. The checked `SourceException` carries it.
    `Permanent` is an answer about the document (it can never be read as it is). `Rejected` is a
    request the provider refused as made (malformed, an unknown field mask). It says nothing about the
    document and is not definitive, so a connector defect or an API change cannot tombstone a corpus.
  - `KnownVersion`, `Partition` and `SourceContext`.
  - `SourceConnectors` is the CDI registry by type, and fails startup when two beans claim one type.
- **Adapters:** `crawl/WebSourceConnector` wraps `WebCrawler` unchanged. Pages arrive prefetched, a
  304 becomes `UNCHANGED`, and crawl errors are classified so that `isDefinitive()` is exactly the
  crawler's `!contentUnknown`. `files/UploadSourceConnector` lists without reading, uses the hash of
  the bytes as the version token, and maps a deleted-meanwhile file to `NotFound` and a store failure
  to `Transient`. The `toCrawlRequest` and upload listing loop moved into the adapters verbatim.
- **Pipeline:** `Collector` no longer implements `CrawlSink`. It processes work items generically.
  Text extraction stays in the framework, selected by content kind, which keeps the
  page-skipped-when-blank and file-failed-when-blank semantics. The old 8-argument constructor
  remains and builds the two connectors. CDI uses a new constructor that takes `SourceConnectors`.
- **Traversal evidence is counted, not inferred.** The blank-site guard (`learnedSomething`) used to
  treat `failed - unreachable` as definitive answers. A source-level failure (no document id) counts
  as failed but was never unreachable, so a `FULL_TRAVERSAL` run that learned nothing about any
  document passed for one that saw the corpus gone, and its absence-based deletion ran. `Collector`
  now counts `definitive` where it is learned: a document-level `NotFound`/`Permanent`, or a file
  with no text. Embed and budget failures no longer stand in for evidence either. That is the safe
  direction, since the only effect is that reconciliation runs less often. The now write-only
  `unreachable` counter is gone.
- **Config models only, not wired:** `SourceFilters` (`formats`, `excludeNamePatterns`,
  `includePaths`, `maxFileBytes`) and `SourcePartitions` (`LISTED` | `ALL_ACCESSIBLE`, `ids`).
- **Docs:** a "Source connectors" section in [`rag.md`](../rag.md#source-connectors-for-contributors).

### Not in this slice

The following are not in F1:

- **Cursors are not stored.** The pipeline passes none and ignores a returned one, so every run
  lists in full.
- **No multi-partition runs.** More than one partition is refused with a run failure.
- **No work queue or workers, and no superseding of item versions.** These are F2 and F3.
- **`DELETE` items** are applied directly: vectors are removed first, then the item is tombstoned.
  No built-in connector emits them yet.

**Files:** [`IngestionPipeline.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/IngestionPipeline.java),
[`connector/`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/connector/SourceConnector.java),
[`WebSourceConnector.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/crawl/WebSourceConnector.java),
[`UploadSourceConnector.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/files/UploadSourceConnector.java),
[`SourceFilters.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/rag/model/SourceFilters.java),
[`SourcePartitions.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/rag/model/SourcePartitions.java)
