## 🔒 fix(cluster): NATS TLS, failing loudly, replication, manifests and alerts (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/cluster-ops-security`) — workstream D of the cluster production-readiness review (`planning/cluster-production-readiness-review.md`, findings C1, C2, C3, C5, C7–C15 and the scheduler starvation of D6).

### What changed and why

- **C1 — TLS host names.** EDDI's hostname-verifying SSL context is now built for every `tls://` or `wss://` server, not only with `eddi.nats.tls.enabled=true`. The chart's external path never set that flag, so jnats fell back to the JVM default context, which accepts any CA-signed certificate for any host: a man in the middle received the shared NATS password. `opentls://` (trust anything) fails the boot. A real-server test (`NatsConnectionSecurityIT`) also found that the previous hostname verification (endpoint identification on the socket) never worked with a host name: jnats resolves the name and dials — and hands TLS — the bare IP, so every certificate issued for the host was refused (and jnats' `Unresolved` mode does not connect at all). Verification now happens in a trust manager ([`ServerNameTrustManager.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/ServerNameTrustManager.java)): the chain must be trusted AND the certificate must name one of the hosts in `eddi.nats.url` (DNS SAN, one leading wildcard label, or an IP SAN for an IP). `HostnameVerifyingSslContext` is gone. [`NatsConnectionManager.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/NatsConnectionManager.java), [`ClusterConfig.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/ClusterConfig.java)
- **C1, reconciled with main (#1249).** Main fixed the same hole with `HostnameVerifyingSslContext` plus jnats `HappyEyeballs` resolution. The merge keeps one check, `ServerNameTrustManager`: it compares the certificate with the configured names whatever address is dialled, so it also covers servers jnats discovers from INFO by IP. From main it keeps `HappyEyeballs` (the handshake carries the name as SNI), the real-handshake `NatsTlsHandshakeTest`, and mutual TLS with the JVM's `javax.net.ssl.keyStore` certificate when no EDDI key store is configured (now loaded explicitly into the EDDI context; a new handshake test covers it).
- **C2 — fail loudly.** A node that never connected since boot, or whose connection fails for a reason retrying cannot heal (authentication, TLS, client options), now reports not-ready (`eddi.cluster.readiness.require-first-connect`, `eddi.cluster.readiness.fail-on-misconfiguration`, both default `true`) instead of serving every turn unfenced while green. Every NATS error is classified and counted in `eddi_cluster_nats_errors_total{cause}` (all causes registered at 0); misconfigurations log at ERROR, the rest at WARN, rate-limited; `eddi_cluster_nats_misconfigured` is a gauge. Trust stores may be PEM (a cert-manager `ca.crt`), and the chart mounts the NATS CA — the in-chart TLS Secret's, or `nats.externalCaSecretName` — into EDDI as its trust store; the in-chart URL is `tls://` with `nats.tls.enabled`. [`ClusterHealthCheck.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/ClusterHealthCheck.java)
- **C5 — dead connections.** `eddi.nats.ping-interval` (5 s) and `eddi.nats.max-pings-out` (2): a server lost without a TCP reset is noticed in about 10–15 s instead of about 4 minutes.
- **C3 — replication.** The chart renders `EDDI_NATS_REPLICAS` for an external NATS too (`nats.externalReplicas`, default 3); EDDI warns at connect when R1 meets three or more servers, or the replica count exceeds the servers it knows.
- **C7 — rollouts.** Readiness still flips at SIGTERM, but turns are admitted for the readiness grace (3 s) before the gate closes; the chart (`eddi.preStopSleepSeconds`, 5) and the Kustomize `nats` component add a `preStop` sleep, counted in the termination-grace check. [`GracefulShutdownService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/runtime/internal/GracefulShutdownService.java)
- **C8 — Kustomize NATS.** The `nats` component now authenticates clients and routes (credentials from a `nats-auth` Secret the operator creates — **existing users must create it before applying**), restricts EDDI's user, sets `max_file_store` with headroom, requires one NATS pod per node, spreads zones, sets `GOMEMLIMIT` and `EDDI_CLUSTER_DEGRADED_TURNS=reject`. TLS remains the Helm chart's.
- **C9 — storage.** `<prefix>_ARCHIVES` gets `eddi.cluster.archives.max-bytes` (1 GiB; an existing bucket is capped on first use); the in-chart `max_file_store` is 80 % of the volume (`nats.storage.maxFileStore` overrides). [`ClusterArchiveStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/ClusterArchiveStore.java)
- **C10 — NATS StatefulSet.** `nats:2.11.17-alpine` pinned by digest on every delivery path (chart, Kustomize, compose; `ThirdPartyImagePinsTest` enforces it), required anti-affinity (`nats.antiAffinity`) plus zone spread, `nats.goMemLimit`, an optional `prometheus-nats-exporter` sidecar with a ServiceMonitor (`nats.metrics.enabled`), and a second user for password rotation without downtime (`nats.auth.secondaryUsername`/`secondaryPassword`).
- **C11 / C4 (chart side) — isolation and least privilege.** The in-chart and Kustomize NATS deny EDDI's user `$JS.API.STREAM.DELETE.>`, `$JS.API.SERVER.>`, `$JS.API.META.>` and `$JS.API.ACCOUNT.PURGE.>` (checked against a real NATS 2.11.17: KV, streams, purge and object store still work; a stream delete is refused). `eddi.nats.inbox-prefix` (default jnats' `_INBOX`) and the opt-in `nats.isolation.enabled` put a deployment in its own NATS account with its own inbox prefix — opt-in because it moves the buckets into a new account.
- **C12 — alerts.** `eddi-cluster-alerts.yml` gains NATS misconfiguration, NATS errors by cause, member loss, lease takeovers, an event-outbox backlog and, from the NATS exporter, JetStream storage, a full dead-letter stream and slow consumers; it has promtool tests (`eddi-cluster-alerts.test.yml`, run in CI) and the chart's PrometheusRule includes it in cluster mode.
- **C13 — ingress.** With `ingress.className: nginx` the chart disables buffering and sets a 300 s read timeout (SSE), and in cluster mode adds an `<release>-mcp` Ingress hashing `/mcp` on the client address (`ingress.nginxDefaults`, `ingress.readTimeoutSeconds`).
- **C14 — HPA.** `autoscaling.targetMemoryUtilizationPercentage` defaults to `null`: a JVM keeps its heap, so the memory target ratcheted the HPA to `maxReplicas`.
- **C15 — configuration.** Boot refuses non-positive durations, two authentication methods at once and a malformed inbox prefix; warnings for an acquire timeout below the lease TTL or at or above the agent timeout, plaintext credentials and `verify-hostname=false`.
- **D6 — scheduler.** The cluster scheduler's two platform threads now only keep time; every job runs on a virtual thread and never overlaps itself ([`OffloadingScheduler.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/OffloadingScheduler.java)), so jobs blocked on NATS or the database can no longer delay presence past its 30 s TTL.
- **Docs:** [Clustering](../clustering.md) (degraded mode, failure modes, permissions, TLS, alerts), [Kubernetes](../kubernetes.md), [Configuration reference](../configuration-reference.md), [Metrics](../metrics.md), the monitoring guide; the Platform Operator explains a not-ready node (revision bump). Helm chart **2.14.0**.

### Design decisions

- Readiness DOWN for a misconfiguration takes every node with the same bad credential out of the load balancer. That is the intended trade: loud instead of silently unfenced. Both switches can be turned off.
- The code default of `degraded.turns` stays `local`; the chart exposes `eddi.cluster.degradedTurns` and the docs and the Kustomize component recommend `reject`.
- Account isolation and a custom inbox prefix are opt-in: switching an existing release would strand its dead letters and archives in the old account.

### Review follow-ups

- Only a certificate or handshake-policy failure counts as a TLS misconfiguration: a handshake
  the peer cut off or a reset connection (a NATS restart) is `io` and no longer turns every pod
  not-ready.
- A failed cap of an existing archive bucket is retried on the next use instead of being given up.
- `helm upgrade --reuse-values` from an older chart no longer fails on the missing
  `nats.externalReplicas` (defaults to 3) or `nats.isolation` / `nats.metrics` maps.
- `EddiClusterMemberLost` compares with the member count that held for 10 minutes, so a rolling
  update's surge pod (3 → 4 → 3) no longer fires it; a promtool case covers that.
- Docs: `require-first-connect` must be off as well to serve from boot; the Kustomize NATS needs
  EDDI switched to `tls://` too, not only a server `tls {}` block; an `eddi.existingSecret`
  release must update that Secret and restart EDDI before the old NATS user is removed. The
  Platform Operator no longer rules out a NATS outage for a node that never connected.
- The NATS image pin test checks the whole reference, not a valid prefix of it. The CI chart
  checks use low-entropy fixture passwords (secret scanning flagged the earlier placeholders).

### What's next

- Per-node NKey/JWT users need an external NATS with account management; the chart stays with password users.
- Signed RPC/events (C4) and the receiving-side last-node guard are workstreams C and B.


- Operator prompt (revision 55): a `config` NATS failure is diagnosed from the reported error detail (credential, trust store or client certificate), and with `eddi.cluster.readiness.require-nats=true` a node that connected and then became degraded is also not ready.

## 🐛 fix(cluster): shared state, events, deployments, node identity, signed control plane (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/cluster-shared-state`) — workstream C of the cluster production-readiness
hardening (findings B1–B5, C4 signing part, C6, C15 versioning part, D6 presence part, D7).

### What changed and why

- **B1 — `autoDeploy=false` deployments are recorded.** They existed only as `deployment.changed`
  events, so a lost undeploy, an outbox that delivered an undeploy before its redeploy, or a node
  that joined later left them deployed (or missing — 404 for about 1/N of the requests) on some
  nodes for good. They now live in a `<prefix>_DEPLOYMENTS` KV bucket as `DEPLOYED` or an
  `UNDEPLOYED` tombstone, ordered by the KV revision; the deployment sweep reconciles them like the
  deployment collection, refreshes them (compare-and-set) while they are served and re-creates an
  expired one only when it was confirmed within the TTL. A deployment event is checked against the
  records before it is applied: an undeploy older than a redeploy, or a transient deploy older than
  its tombstone, is dropped. Not in the database, as the review suggested: the record must vanish
  once the whole cluster is gone (that is what "not recorded" means), and a new status value in the
  deployment collection would have broken older nodes that parse it during a rolling upgrade.
- **B2 — events.** A node resyncs once after its first subscription (it subscribes at
  `DeliverPolicy.New`, so whatever was published while it booted with NATS down never arrived),
  and when the event stream was recreated — detected by its creation time (checked every 30 s) or
  by a sequence that goes back — it re-subscribes and resyncs.
- **B3 — cache events.** A read-through fill (`ICache.fill` / `fillIfAbsent`) is no longer
  announced as an eviction; only mutations are. Dispatch runs on two bounded lanes (cache
  evictions vs everything else), so a burst of evictions no longer delays a secret rotation; a
  full lane drops the event and resyncs.
- **B4 — webhook de-duplication fails closed** while NATS is unreachable under
  `eddi.cluster.degraded.nonces=reject` (the default): 503, the sender retries. The docs now list
  the cache TTLs of secrets (5 min), connections (1 min) and global variables (2 min) and no longer
  claim that security state always fails closed.
- **B5.** A GDPR stop waits for the member count it read itself, with its own timeout
  (`eddi.cluster.gdpr-stop-timeout`, 10 s), and the user-id hash travels sealed (AES-GCM, key derived
  from the signing key) on the RPC, on `gdpr.user-erased` and in cache-eviction keys when a signing
  key is set. Audit-position counters that are (re)seeded are counted (`outcome=seeded`).
- **C4 (signing part) — signed control plane.** With `eddi.cluster.signing.key` every node RPC, RPC
  reply and cluster event carries an `Eddi-Sig` header: HMAC-SHA256 over the subject, a timestamp, a
  random nonce and the SHA-256 of the body, compared in constant time. Without a key a WARN is logged
  and nothing changes; `eddi.cluster.signing.required=true` refuses unsigned messages and refuses to
  start without a key. Configuration lives in the new `ClusterSigningConfig`, not `ClusterConfig`.
- **C6 — duplicate node ids.** The presence record is written with compare-and-set; a node whose key
  is held by another boot waits one interval and takes over only a record that stopped changing
  (a crashed predecessor). A live twin stops announcing itself, logs an ERROR, reports
  `eddi_cluster_node_duplicate 1` and its new `cluster-node-identity` readiness check is DOWN.
  Falling back to `eddi.schedule.instance-id` as the node id now logs a WARN.
- **C15 (versioning part).** RPCs and events carry their payload version (`Eddi-V`, event `v`); an
  unversioned message is version 1; a newer one is handled best-effort and counted. Presence records
  carry the payload protocol, and members on mixed versions log a WARN (`eddi_cluster_versions`).
- **D6 (presence part).** Members come from a KV watch on the presence bucket instead of listing it
  and reading every record every few seconds on every node.
- **D7.** The event stream is updated only when its configuration differs, and a cluster-wide
  resync starts after a random delay (`eddi.cluster.events.resync-jitter`, 5 s).

### Threat model of the signing

Who is kept out: anyone who holds the shared NATS credentials (every pod has them) or can read and
write NATS traffic, but not the signing key. Such a party could drain every node, stop a user's
work, undeploy agents or trigger resync storms; with a key, a message must carry a valid HMAC made
with it. A signature is bound to the subject and the body hash, so it cannot be moved to another
operation or payload. Replay is bounded twice: a request older (or further ahead) than
`eddi.cluster.signing.max-age` (60 s) is refused, and within that window a nonce already seen is
refused; an event's window is the event retention plus that, because a reconnecting node
legitimately receives events that old. A reply is bound to the nonce of its request, so an old
answer cannot be passed off as a new one. Not covered: confidentiality (use NATS TLS), the KV and
stream state (per-node NATS permissions, workstream D), flooding NATS, and a holder of the key. The
nonce memory is per node and in memory: a node that restarts inside the window could accept one
replay of a message it had already seen, which the 60 s window keeps short.

### Design decisions

- Rolling upgrade first: unsigned and unversioned messages from older nodes stay accepted unless
  `eddi.cluster.signing.required=true`; a node without a key accepts signed messages it cannot
  check. The three-step order is in [Clustering](../clustering.md#rolling-upgrades).
- The audit-counter margin the review recommends was **not** added: a margin is a gap in the
  conversation's chain, which verification grades `BROKEN`. A re-seed is counted instead, so a
  duplicate position can be attributed.

### Needs from other workstreams

- WS-A: `KvLeaseManager` should not sweep or take over "our earlier boot" leases while
  `ClusterPresence.isDuplicate()` is true (a twin still runs its lease sweeps).
- WS-B: `RestAgentAdministration` keeps calling `announceTransientDeployment`; nothing else needed.

### Review follow-ups

- **The boot sweep waits for presence to confirm the node id.** On a twin's first connect presence
  has not yet told a live twin from a dead predecessor (that takes one interval), so the
  duplicate flag was still down and the sweep deleted the live twin's leases. The sweep, and the
  own-node-id takeover, now need `ClusterView.isNodeIdentityConfirmed()` (this JVM holds its own
  presence record and no twin is flagged); `ClusterPresence.onIdentityConfirmed` re-runs the
  sweep when that happens.
- **A failed event re-subscribe is retried by the periodic stream check**, which resyncs once it
  succeeds. Before, a subscribe that failed after a stream recreation while the connection stayed
  up left the node deaf until the next reconnect.
- **Key rotation and sealed GDPR hashes.** A rotated node also opens hashes sealed with a key in
  `previous-keys`; a node still on the old key cannot open the new key's, so `clustering.md` now
  says to re-run erasures made during the rolling window.
- `eddi-cluster-alerts.yml` gains the `EddiClusterNodeDuplicate` and `EddiClusterSigningRejected`
  rules `metrics.md` already promised. The presence watch drops records past the TTL instead of
  only skipping them, and the signed-event test tampers with the timestamp and the nonce too. Test fixture keys are low-entropy strings (a hex-looking
  key tripped secret scanning).
- **Second round.** A presence takeover needs one full interval of (monotonic) clock time, not
  two writes in a row — a reconnect and a scheduled tick can come milliseconds apart and would
  have confirmed the id over a live twin. A create lost after the own record expired resets the
  own revision, so the node is no longer "confirmed" without a record. The
  `eddi.cluster.signing.rejected` series are registered at zero on a node that can refuse
  anything, so `EddiClusterSigningRejected` fires on the first refusal. A failed subscribe retry
  falls through to the stream check, which recreates a missing stream; a resync the full control
  lane refuses is kept and retried after a second instead of waiting for the next request.

**Files:** [`TransientDeployments.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/TransientDeployments.java),
[`AgentDeploymentManagement.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/runtime/internal/AgentDeploymentManagement.java),
[`DeploymentStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/deployment/mongo/DeploymentStore.java),
[`JetStreamEventBus.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/events/JetStreamEventBus.java),
[`ClusterMessageSigner.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/ClusterMessageSigner.java),
[`ClusterSigningConfig.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/ClusterSigningConfig.java),
[`NatsClusterRpc.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/rpc/NatsClusterRpc.java),
[`ClusterPresence.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/ClusterPresence.java),
[`NodeIdentityHealthCheck.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/NodeIdentityHealthCheck.java),
[`ClusterInvalidatingCache.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/caching/ClusterInvalidatingCache.java),
[`CacheFactory.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/caching/CacheFactory.java),
[`WebhookEventHandler.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/integrations/webhook/WebhookEventHandler.java),
[`AuditClusterSupport.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/AuditClusterSupport.java),
[`GdprComplianceService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/gdpr/GdprComplianceService.java);
docs [Clustering](../clustering.md), [Configuration Reference](../configuration-reference.md),
[Metrics](../metrics.md), [Security](../security.md), [GDPR](../gdpr-compliance.md).

## 🐛 fix(cluster): fence first, never lower the fence, dead-letter what was refused (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/cluster-turn-fencing`)

Workstream B of the cluster production-readiness review (`planning/cluster-production-readiness-review.md`):
the turn path, the conversation stores, HITL resume, dead-letter replay and the admin console.

### What changed and why

- **A3, fence first.** A turn under a cluster lease raised the conversation's `_fence` only *after*
  it had read the state, the revision, a reloaded snapshot and the recorded `Idempotency-Key`s, so an
  expired predecessor could still append in between: the successor then ran without that step, its
  properties overwrote the predecessor's, and a duplicate `Idempotency-Key` could run twice. A failed
  raise was only logged. Now the fence goes up before anything is read, and a turn that cannot raise
  it — or whose lease is already lost — does not run: 409 + `Retry-After`, input not consumed. The
  HITL resume is ordered differently: it reads the snapshot and moves the state `AWAITING_HUMAN` →
  `IN_PROGRESS` on the request thread, and only its queued task raises the fence, before the resumed
  pipeline runs; a resume that cannot raise it does not run and restores the pause.
  `ConversationStepRunnerLeaseTest.raiseFailureDoesNotStopTheTurn` asserted the old behaviour and was
  replaced by `raiseFailureAnswersBusy`.
- **A4, the fence only grows.** A full-document write (Mongo `replaceOne`, Postgres `data = ?`)
  persisted the `_fence` it had loaded. A raise does not move `_rev`, so an unfenced writer that
  loaded before a raise passed the revision guard and put the old fence back, after which the holder
  the raise had shut out could write again. Postgres now builds `_fence` in SQL (`GREATEST` for a
  fenced write, the stored value for an unfenced one); Mongo refuses to write a lower fence and adopts
  the stored one. Documents without `_fence` stay writable. Undo and redo, which ran on the REST
  thread outside the lease, now run through the coordinator in cluster mode, under the lease, with
  its token on their write (single-node undo/redo are unchanged). A turn whose write lost the
  optimistic-concurrency race after its reply had gone out is dead-lettered in cluster mode
  (`reason: conflict`) instead of only logged.
- **A6.** A conditional write (pause commit, resume, undo, redo) refused by the fence returned
  `false`, which every caller read as "a concurrent writer moved the state": the turn was dropped
  without a dead letter. It now throws `ConversationFencedException`; the say path and the resume
  dead-letter it, undo/redo answer 409.
- **A5.** A HITL resume that lost its lease only raised the cancel flag, and nothing then moved the
  conversation off `IN_PROGRESS` until crash recovery minutes later. The pause is now restored at
  once with a fenced compare-and-set (new `IConversationMemoryStore.compareAndSetStateFenced`: only
  while no newer holder has started on the conversation) and the resume is dead-lettered; its caller
  is answered 409.
- **A1 (turn side).** The turn's memory reads `LeaseHandle.isLost()` itself
  (`IConversationMemory.setLeaseLostCheck`), so a lease that can no longer be trusted cancels the
  turn, skips the unfenced long-term user-memory write and answers the reply as not processed, even
  before the lease manager's lost-callback arrives. When `isLost()` turns true is the lease
  workstream's part (`fix/cluster-lease-validity`).
- **A7.** A replay deleted its dead letter as soon as the turn was *queued*, so a replayed turn that
  was then skipped, timed out on the lease or failed lost its input. The entry is now deleted only
  once the replayed turn commits (checked through its `Idempotency-Key`, `dead-letter-replay:<id>`,
  which every replay of one entry carries), the claim is held until then, and the outcome is on the
  activity timeline. With turn idempotency switched off there is no key to look for, and the check
  falls back to "the conversation's revision moved past the one read before the replay" — another
  turn can move it too, so in that mode a deleted entry does not prove the replay itself committed.
  The legacy `POST /administration/coordinator/dead-letters/{id}/replay` goes
  through the same path, so it now claims the entry and is audited with the calling admin.
- **A9.** A turn the runtime refused after its lease was granted was both dead-lettered and answered
  "retry". It is answered busy, and its dead letter is marked `answeredRetry` and cannot be replayed
  (`notReplayableReason: ANSWERED_RETRY`).
- **A11 (group lease).** A discussion whose `g.<id>` lease another live node holds no longer runs a
  second leg here; it stops without writing. Only when no holder can be read (NATS unreachable) does
  a discussion run without its lease. `ClusterHitlRecovery` no longer takes a lease read made while
  NATS is unreachable for "nobody holds it, recover now" (it checks the connection around the read
  until the lease workstream's `peekStrict` is wired in).
- **C4 (receiving side).** A drain request that arrives over RPC is checked again on the node it
  drains: the drain gate must be held, its own `drain.<node>` key written, and another live node must
  still take turns — so a request published straight onto the RPC subject, a broadcast included,
  drains nothing. What a node applies or refuses on another node's request (drain, undrain,
  reconcile, forward) is written to its audit ledger (actor `cluster-rpc`) and activity timeline.
- **D6 (console reads).** One scan of the lease bucket and one read of each dead-letter page is
  shared for 3 s by every console polling this node, and dropped by a force-release, replay, discard
  or forward.
- **C12 (part).** `eddi.cluster.fence.rejected` is registered at startup, so the first rejection on
  a pod is visible to `increase()`.
- **A8, A10 (docs).** `clustering.md` no longer claims "no loss" for degraded turns (conversation
  properties are last-writer-wins), nor that node-local dead letters survive a restart or the
  1000-entry cap.

### Design decisions

- Mongo cannot express `$max` inside a `replaceOne`, so the full write is filtered on "the stored
  fence is not above the one written" and, on a miss caused only by that, adopts the stored fence and
  tries again (at most three times). Only an unfenced write can miss this way.
- Undo/redo go through the coordinator only in cluster mode: that is where the fence exists; on one
  node the state-and-revision CAS already orders them.
- The node-local dead-letter ring (A10) is not persisted to disk here: it lives in the coordinator
  base class, and a durable journal would also have to honour GDPR erasure. The docs now say what it
  does; persisting it is left to the coordinator workstream.

### Review follow-ups

- A resume that lost its lease re-arms the pause's timeout from the **stored** bookmark: the
  resumed pipeline had already cleared the live memory's, so a finite `AUTO_REJECT` /
  `AUTO_APPROVE` / `ABORT` policy was left without a schedule until the next restart.
- A force-release drops the shared lease scan *after* the release, not before, and a scan that
  read the bucket while an invalidation happened is returned but not cached (generation counter),
  so the console does not show a released lease as held for 3 s.
- `clustering.md` no longer calls the group `g.<id>` lease exclusive: the discussion's writes are
  not fenced and a holder that loses the lease is not stopped. The A3 and A7 entries above now
  state the resume's real fencing order and the replay check's fallback without idempotency.

**Files:** [`ConversationStepRunner.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/ConversationStepRunner.java),
[`ConversationService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/ConversationService.java),
[`ConversationHitlService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/ConversationHitlService.java),
[`ConversationMemoryStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/ConversationMemoryStore.java),

## Log capture starts at boot; unauthenticated reminder is opt-in (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI, branch `fix/log-capture-at-startup`

**Defect.** `/administration/logs` and `/administration/logs/history` returned nothing on a production instance that had been serving conversations for hours; capture began at the first request to the log API. `BoundedLogStore` was a plain `@ApplicationScoped` bean that registered itself with `LogCaptureFilter` in `@PostConstruct`, but it was only injected by `RestLogAdmin` and `McpConversationTools`, so it was created on first use. Until then `LogCaptureFilter` (which forwards only when a store is registered) fed nothing to the ring buffer, the SSE tail or the DB persistence, and every startup warning was lost from EDDI's own log view after each restart. Console output was never affected.

**Fix.**
- `BoundedLogStore` is now `@Startup`, so the ring buffer, DB flush scheduler and shutdown flush run from application start.
- `LogCaptureFilter` holds records logged before a store exists in a bounded pre-start buffer (1000, newest kept, drops counted) and replays them in order, plus a WARN naming any dropped count, when `setStore` is called. Only redacted text is buffered; a record whose redaction fails is never buffered raw and is still stripped for the console. Console output is unchanged. Javadoc "Bootstrap Safety" updated.
- Docs: `docs/log-administration.md` states capture starts at boot.

**Unauthenticated reminder.** `AuthStartupGuard` logged a WARN `REMINDER: OIDC is DISABLED` every hour for deployments that knowingly set `eddi.security.allow-unauthenticated=true`. It now logs the `[SECURITY]` ERROR once at startup (wording and level unchanged) and the repeat is opt-in through `eddi.security.unauthenticated-reminder-interval` (default `off`; a duration such as `1h` enables it; repeats closer than 5 minutes are throttled). Documented in `application.properties`, `docs/configuration-reference.md`, `docs/security.md`, `docs/troubleshooting.md`, `docs/docker.md`, `docs/architecture.md`.

**Tests.** `LogCaptureFilterPreStartTest` (eager store, ordered replay with MDC, bounded buffer, redaction, fail-closed, live path) and extended `AuthStartupGuardTest`.

**Brand-leak E2E.** With capture running from boot, the Logs page now shows startup records that name the engine, which `brand-leak.fullstack.spec.ts` flagged. Log message and stacktrace text is server output, not product chrome: `LogRow` (Logs page) and the live log viewer mark it `data-log-content`, and the spec's snapshot skips that text and its attributes while still scanning the rest of the page. `docs/white-labeling.md` "Not branded" notes it.

**Review follow-ups.** A plain JUL `LogRecord` with parameters whose message redaction left unchanged was skipped by the pre-start buffer: `LogRecordRedactor` hands back no reusable message for that shape (capture formats it printf-first), and the filter read that as a redaction failure. The filter now formats it with `BoundedLogStore.formatRecord` (now package-private), redacts it with `LogRecordRedactor.rewrite` and buffers it; two tests cover the printf and MessageFormat cases. `docs/architecture.md`'s auth table no longer promises an hourly WARN (it is opt-in), and `docs/log-administration.md` names what the ring buffer (`eddi.logs.buffer-size`), database history (`db-enabled`, `db-persist-min-level`, retention) and the live tail (50 recent entries on connect) keep of startup records. The brand-leak spec gains a server-free `page.setContent` test proving the `data-log-content` exclusion.

## 🔒 fix(deps): minimatch 11.0.4 in Manager and Chat UI — Dependabot #75 and #76 (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/minimatch-dos-alerts`)

Both alerts (minimatch globparts amplification DoS, patched in 11.0.1) were dev-only transitive dependencies
through ESLint and typescript-eslint, which pin `^10`, and in the Manager also through
`@stryker-mutator/core`, which pins `~10.2.4`. A lockfile refresh alone could not reach the fix, so
`ui/manager/package.json` and `ui/chat/package.json` gain an `overrides` entry `"minimatch": "^11.0.1"`, and both
lockfiles resolve to 11.0.4 (new transitive `@isaacs/nonrecursive-glob-match`). The lockfiles were edited by hand
rather than regenerated on Windows, which drops the `libc` and `peer` fields. `npm ci` and ESLint pass in both
packages.

**Decision:** the override is a major bump for tooling only; remove it only once none of ESLint,
typescript-eslint and (Manager only) Stryker selects an affected `minimatch` 10.x range any more.

**Review follow-ups:** the dependency summary and the removal condition now name Stryker, whose `~10.2.4`
range would otherwise bring an affected 10.x back the moment the override is dropped.

## feat(integration): F9 — the cross-feature fixes, MCP tools and Platform Operator for the ten feature PRs (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/operator-mcp-integration`)

The combined follow-up to cost accounting, team handoff, the SERVICE judge, human tie-breaks, human task executors,
tool-call guardrails, edit-on-approve, approval analytics and earned autonomy, approval card context and team
budgets. Those ten PRs deliberately left out what every one of them would have conflicted on — new MCP tools and
the Platform Operator — and wrote down what each needed in `planning/operator-followups/<feature>.md`. This entry is
what was built from those notes, after checking each against the merged code.

### Cross-feature fixes

- **A handoff into a team that cannot pay is refused.** `TeamBudgetHandoffAdmission` is the first
  `HandoffAdmission` bean: the *receiving* team's `TeamBudgetService.decide` (spent, tenant spent, or unreadable
  — fail closed) refuses the handoff with the budget's own sentence, before anything is filed. New refusal surface
  `handoff` on `eddi_team_budget_refusals_total`. See [Team Budgets](../team-budgets.md#what-happens-at-the-cap)
  and [Cross-Team Handoff](../team-handoff.md).
- **A follow-up respects the team ceiling and is charged.** `GroupConversationService.resolveEffectiveCostCeiling`
  ignored `teamBudgetState.ceilingUsd`, so a follow-up on a discussion that had already reached its team's ceiling
  ran one more paid turn; and a follow-up starts no leg, so its spend reached the team only if the discussion was
  ever continued. The ceiling now includes the team's, and `GroupLifecycleOps.followUpWithMember` charges the turn
  as soon as its cost is harvested, as a phase turn does.
- **The SERVICE judge prices from the catalog.** `JudgeCallPricing` was the seam left for the price resolver; it now
  passes the judge's `llmProvider/llmModel` to `ModelPriceResolver`, so a judge without prices of its own is priced
  from the [price catalog](../model-pricing.md) instead of $0. Explicit prices still win.
- **Dashboard.** The HITL row of the Full Metrics Reference had two panels at the same position after the merge
  (approval-context renders and decision latency); the former moved below. The k8s copies are regenerated.
- **Schema version.** `handoffState` and `teamBudgetState` ride `GroupConversation` schema v5 with the tie-break
  fields — recorded in the version comment as decided, valid while the ten ship in one release.
- `GroupConversationService.executeTaskExecutionPhase` keeps its unused `speakers` parameter (the reflection seam
  every phase delegator shares); the reason is now in its Javadoc.

### MCP tools (96 → 108)

Thin wrappers over the in-process REST beans, so every access rule of the REST resource — existence first, VIEW or
EDIT on the group, per-agent oversight scoping, the caller's principal as the recorded actor — applies to the MCP
caller unchanged. Each class checks its tier itself, because an in-process call never passes the HTTP layer's
`@RolesAllowed`; `McpRoleTierParityTest` measures the tiers and compares them with the REST annotations (now also
method-level ones). New tiers in `McpRoles`: `OVERSIGHT` (admin, editor, approver) and `BUDGET_READ` (admin, editor,
viewer). `McpToolUtils.responseJson` turns a resource's `Response` into the tool result, keeping its sentence or code.

| Class | Tools | Tier |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `McpPricingTools` | `read_model_prices(provider?, model?)` | Author (read only — prices are a budget lever) |
| `McpTeamHandoffTools` | `list_team_handoffs`, `accept_team_handoff`, `decline_team_handoff` | Author, then VIEW / EDIT on the group |
| `McpTeamBudgetTools` | `read_team_budget(groupId, historyMonths?)`, `list_team_budgets` | Budget read |
| | `adjust_team_budget`, `transfer_team_budget` | Admin |
| `McpOversightTools` | `summarize_approvals`, `list_autonomy_recommendations`, `get_autonomy_state`, `count_pending_audit_samples` | Oversight |

Not built: a tool to file a handoff or delete a backlog task, any oversight write (never), the per-decision and
sample listings (they carry users' redacted tool arguments), `preview_approval_context` (no render-preview service
method exists) and a separate `get_hitl_decisions` (`summarize_approvals` answers it). The `eddi-admin` Claude Code
skill teaches the new tools (`SKILL.md`, `references/groups.md`, `references/destructive-ops.md`).

**Found on the way:** `RestTeamHandoffs` caught the guard's `io.quarkus.security.ForbiddenException` in its generic
`catch (Exception)` and answered **500** to a caller without access to the group, over REST and MCP alike; its test
threw the JAX-RS `ForbiddenException`, which the guard never throws. It now rethrows the Quarkus exception (403), and
the test uses the real type. `RestGroupWorkspace` on `main` has the same pattern and is left for its own fix.

### Platform Operator (revision 52 → 53)

- **Allow-list** (`tool-scopes.ts`), reads only, each with the exclusions written next to it: the price catalog,
  model resolution and unpriced sightings, and one tenant's usage (`PUT …/model-prices`, the reference list and every
  quota write stay out — a price is a budget lever); a team's handoff intake inbox (accept, decline and backlog
  delete stay out — an intake decision is a person's); a team's budget, its history and the cross-team listing (the
  cap change and the transfer stay out); the approval analytics summary, the earned-autonomy states, one category's
  evidence and the sample count (the sample list, the per-decision rows and every oversight write stay out). Four
  new predicates gate the matching prompt sections, so a deployment without one of the reads is not told about it.
- **Prompt** (`system-prompt.ts`): sections on cost and prices (resolution order, unpriced models and the strict
  policy, where cost shows), cross-team handoff (two-sided consent, REVIEW vs AUTO, why group handoffs are gated on
  the receiving side, every refusal), team budgets (window, who pays, every refusal, the admin-only calls to give)
  and approval oversight (tiers, `sampledAudit`, `autonomyPolicy`, the 409s); and quick-reference entries for the
  SERVICE judge, human tie-breaks, humans in task forces, tool-call guardrails, edit-on-approve with the decision
  audit, and approval card context, plus their documentation pages. The operator is told it never answers a
  tie-break or a human task, never decides an intake item, and never changes a price, a cap or an oversight state.
- **Gate guard** (`gate-guard.ts`): an LLM-config write that sets `toolCallGuardrails` — in a PUT body at any depth,
  or a patch path, `from` or value — is refused in the approval UI with the new reason `carries-guardrails` (string
  `operator.approval.blockedGuardrailCarrying`, all 11 locales), mirroring the backend's `SelfUngatingGuard`. The
  agent-store oversight fields the approval-analytics follow-up asked for need no rule: the operator holds no
  agent-store write.

The ten `planning/operator-followups/*.md` notes stay: planning documents are kept in this repository after they
are done, and these record why each read was granted or left out.

CI's `Integration Tests` job failed the JaCoCo gate (branches covered ratio 0.79, minimum 0.80) because the approval

**Repo:** EDDI (`docs/release-6.7.0-prep`)

### What changed and why

`docs/release-notes.md` stopped at 6.5.0, so 6.6.0 (tagged 2026-10-20) had no row and the 6.5 upgrade
guide was not listed under "Upgrade guides". It now has rows for 6.6.0 and 6.7.0 and lists the guide.

6.7 PRs (cluster hardening, team budgets, HITL review/oversight, cost accounting, the update check,
the review sweep) all merge: changed defaults, fail-closed behaviour, the cluster/NATS/Helm notes,
rolling-upgrade cautions and the opt-in feature switches.

set the 6.7.0 date in the release-notes row, confirm main's `ci.yml` is green, tag `6.7.0` (no `v`).

## feat(streaming): resume stream, tool_result events, stored call identity, toolDisplay (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/resume-stream-tool-events`)

### What changed and why

Streaming clients (a live chat with plain-language activity lines and an approval card) could stream a `say` but not the resume of a human-approval pause, saw a tool start but never finish, and lost call identity in the stored trace, so a receipt could not be rebuilt after a reload.

- **`POST /agents/{conversationId}/resume/stream`** (SSE, same roles, body and query flags as `/resume`). Admission is the plain `/resume`'s own method (`RestAgentEngine.resumeStreaming`), so every refusal (400/403/404/409/503, pause mismatch, secret-context error) is the same plain HTTP error and nothing is written to the stream first; the keep-alive starts only after the decision was accepted. `ConversationHitlService.resumeConversationStreaming` installs the same event sink a streamed `say` uses on the resumed memory; failure, lease loss and thrown errors go to `onError`, a dropped resume ends with a typed `error` (`conversation_ended`, `awaiting_approval`, `conversation_busy`, `resume_dropped`), and a re-pause is still delivered only after it is persisted. `hitl_resume {pauseId, verdict}` is sent once, from the resume callable, before any event of the continued turn. `/resume/stream` follows `eddi.streaming.resume-cancel-on-client-disconnect` (default `false`: a dropped client never discards an approved turn); `/stream` keeps `eddi.streaming.cancel-on-client-disconnect`.
- **Events (additive):** `tool_call {tool, callId, displayName?, display?}`; new `tool_result {tool, callId, status: ok|error|not_executed|rejected, durationMs}`, emitted once per call from a single `try/finally` in `ToolLoopRunner.executeToolCall` and for declined/skipped gated calls from `ToolLoopResumer`; new `approval_required {pauseId, pauseType, callCount}` right before `done` on `/stream` and `/resume/stream` when the turn ends `AWAITING_HUMAN`. `ConversationEventSink` and `StreamingResponseHandler` gained default overloads, so existing implementors (group member observers, `toolCallObserver`) are untouched.
- **Stored trace identity:** `tool_call`, `tool_result` and `tool_error` entries carry `callId`; approved gated calls add `hitl_approved {tool, callId}`; `hitl_rejected` adds the per-call reviewer note and the call's arguments, redacted with `SecretRedactionFilter`.
- **`toolDisplay`** (new optional field on the LLM task): `{"<tool>": {"label": "...", "fields": ["..."]}}`. `label` static, at most 120 characters; `fields` an allow-list of at most 3 top-level argument names; only string/number/boolean values are shown, redacted and cut to 80 characters. Validated at save time (`ToolDisplayConfigValidator`, wired in `LlmStore`), keyed like `toolQuotas`.

### Design decisions

- `task_complete.toolTrace` already carries redacted arguments and results. The new events do not, but a client that forwards events to end users must still drop `toolTrace`; this is now stated in `rest-api-reference.md`, `hitl.md` and `langchain.md`.
- `hitl_resume` is emitted when the resume callable starts rather than at submit, so a resume the coordinator drops (pause restored) never announces itself.
- A streamed `say` that is skipped keeps its old `done` answer; only the resume stream turns a skipped resume into a typed `error` event.
- `onToolCall(String)` keeps working: the new three-argument form defaults to it.

### Files

[`RestAgentEngineStreaming.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/RestAgentEngineStreaming.java), [`ConversationHitlService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/ConversationHitlService.java), [`ToolLoopRunner.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/ToolLoopRunner.java), [`ToolLoopResumer.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/ToolLoopResumer.java), [`ToolDisplayConfigValidator.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/tools/ToolDisplayConfigValidator.java), [`hitl.md`](../hitl.md)

## Review-thread sweep over the merged PR backlog (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI · **Branch:** `fix/review-threads-sweep`

Fixes the findings that were still open as review threads on the 38 PRs merged on 2026-11-02, each checked against current `main` first. Findings that were not defects, or are design work, are answered on their PRs and listed at the end.

**Security and data integrity**

- **Authorization** (`RestWorkflowStore.java`): `addReference` no longer skips a host-bearing reference without a scheme (`//ai.labs.llm/…`); the engine resolves it by host and id, so it now gets the same VIEW check as `eddi://`.
- **Redaction** (`SecretRedactionFilter.java`, `SecretScrubber.java`): a quoted cookie value (`sid="…"`, also as `sid=\"…\"` inside a JSON carrier, with an apostrophe inside, or starting with a colon) is masked with its quotes kept; `Set-Cookie` attribute values (`Path`, `Domain`, `Expires`, …) are exempt only in a `Set-Cookie` header and only after its first pair (the first pair is the cookie itself, even when it is named `Path`), not in a `Cookie` request header, and a bare flag no longer ends the walk; both export-scrubber query paths (relative and absolute URL) judge a percent-encoded name (`%6bey`, `%73ig`) by its decoded form.
- **PDF budget** (`PdfDecodeBudget.java`, `PdfTextExtractor.java`): predictor dimensions are checked for overflow and non-positive values before they are multiplied (a zero dimension also hangs PDFBox's TIFF predictor), and a `/Filter` that PDFBox reads as zero filters (`[]`, a number, a string) is charged like an unfiltered stream.
- **Crawler** (`WebCrawler.java`): the link-length bound is applied to the URL that will be fetched, for page links and sitemap entries alike, before canonicalization drops tracking parameters.
- **Erasure race** (`ClusterConversationCoordinator.java`): after publishing a node-local dead letter the coordinator looks again for an erasure marker and retries until it can — an unreadable marker or a failed purge no longer leaves the erased turn in the shared stream, and a failed purge no longer escapes the fixed-delay forwarding task (which cancelled every later run).
- **Alerts** (`AlertMaintenanceService.java`, both alert event stores): the bounded orphan scan resumes after the last rule id it saw, so rules that still digest cannot crowd a disabled or deleted rule out of every pass.
- **HITL journal** (`HitlToolJournalStore.java`): `reconcileRetention` counts only index drops that happened, still handles every other stale index and recreates the partial one when a drop fails, and then rethrows the first failure (anything but "index not found").
- **Helm** (`helm/eddi`, chart `2.13.0` → `2.14.0`): `nats.externalAllowPlaintext` must be a boolean (the string `"false"` is truthy and switched the plaintext guard off); external NATS credentials are escaped for the properties file they are written to (backslash, line breaks, `${…}` expansion and a leading blank).
- **Audit evidence** (`TurnAuditBuffer.java`): a turn that recorded a failed task, or whose entry the ledger refused, no longer hands its compiled prompt and model response over to the ledger, and a step marker (`audit:evidence_retained`) carries that across a pause and its resume. The failure entry carries no LLM detail, so releasing them deleted the only copy.
- **Legacy path rewrite** (`LegacyPathRewriteFilter.java`): the rules are matched below `quarkus.http.root-path`; the cache memo of hashed keys (`CacheFactory.java`) forgets an evicted key on the publishing node.
- **Shutdown** (`SchedulePollerService.java`): a poll that is already running stops claiming once shutdown begins.

**Correctness and smaller fixes**

- Postgres `loadConversationMemorySnapshot` calls `ensureSchema()` again (a read can be the first statement on a fresh database); the simple projection of a drifted document with outputs and no steps no longer throws.
- Dark-only brand colours render the same stylesheet in mock mode as the server (`brand-css.ts`); `SwaggerUiBranding` survives a malformed alpha; the Slack refusal WARN (`ChannelTargetRouter.java`) carries `[SECURITY]` as documented, and only that one; the apicalls `authHeaderRef` error names `${eddivault:…}`.
- `docs-site/scripts/check-live.mjs` validates both URL arguments; the cluster demo treats an empty `MOCK_LLM_BIND` as unset (it bound every interface); the changelog collator refuses a link to a dated pending fragment (judged on the resolved path and the link destination alone, so `.././changelog.d/…`, a root-relative form and a titled link are caught; the README is still linkable) and the CI register-row detector accepts the same whitespace the collator does.
- **Manager:** the synthesis heading and footer keep their contrast for a bright brand; `BrandIcon` falls back once when `/branding/icon` fails; version labels and the About entry are translated, with focus returned to the Help trigger; French and Korean strings no longer attach an elision or particle to the brand name; the European Portuguese login-theme strings; `swatchFor` shows a valid `rgb()` primary; save, reset, upload and remove on the branding page are one write at a time and bounded by a timeout, and out-of-order or failing live brand scripts can no longer leave a stale or missing brand; a failed logo or icon no longer falls back to EDDI's own wordmark on a branded deployment; `AboutDialog`, `BrandWordmark` and `BrandIcon` are in the design-system mirror.
- **Spent team budget on the group endpoints** (`RestGroupConversation.java`, found in a smoke test of `main` on 2026-11-05): `TeamBudgetRefusedException` is now caught before its parent `GroupDiscussionException` and answers **409 with the refusal's own text** (team, spend, cap, reset, what an administrator can do) on `discuss`, `continue` and `followup`; the streaming start and a streamed continuation send that text in their `group_error` event. Before, `discuss` fell through to a 500, continue and follow-up returned the canned "must be COMPLETED" conflict text on a completed conversation, and the stream said only "could not be started". [`team-budgets.md`](../team-budgets.md) now names the streaming behaviour. `RestGroupConversationTeamBudgetTest` asserts status and body on all four endpoints.
- **Docs:** the vault fallback counter is described as cumulative and attempt-based (rate, not zero total); the manual-fire timeout (`504`, interrupt, 30 s grace); NATS certificates and discovered brokers; a failed Keycloak admin login stops the Compose stack on loopback too; the audit ledger's crash window.

**Answered, not changed**

- `TemplateSyntaxMigrator` ReDoS: already bounded by `conversionWork` before any pattern runs; a regression test pins it.
- `BoundedRegex` stacked counted quantifiers: measured at milliseconds on this JDK, so no refusal is needed.
- `TurnAuditBuffer` durability window: documented; releasing the evidence keys only after the store confirms the entries is a larger change, left open.
- NATS `tls://` without `eddi.nats.tls.enabled` (#1206): superseded on `main` by #1249, which builds the host-name-verifying context for `tls://` and `wss://` URLs too and dials the name itself (`HostnameResolveMode.HappyEyeballs`), with handshake tests (a certificate whose only SAN is the configured host connects; a trusted certificate for another host is refused). A first attempt in this branch gave those URLs the verifying context without that, which would have compared the certificate with a resolved IP; it was withdrawn, and this PR does not touch the NATS TLS code. Only the certificate guidance for servers learned from cluster discovery is added to `docs/clustering.md`.
- `CacheFactory`: receiving nodes keep the memoised hash of an evicted key until the bounded memo turns it over; a reverse lookup per event on the dispatch thread would cost what the memo exists to save.
- A fragment dated 2026-11-01 for the Node 26 change: the date is when it was written.

## ✨ feat(groups): team learning loop — bets and outcomes (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/team-learning-loop`), built on the local integration branch `feat/operator-mcp-integration` because
PRs #1222–#1231 are not merged yet

### What changed and why

Approval gates record that a human said yes; nothing recorded what came of it, and a backlog task was free text. Two
opt-in pieces close that:

- **Bets as first-class backlog tasks.** `TaskItem.bet` (`hypothesis`, `metric`, `target`, `reviewDate`, `reviewedAt`)
  on workspace backlog tasks, filed through REST, `add_team_task`, `addGroupTask`, `handoff_to_team` (and kept in the
  REVIEW inbox until accepted) and the Manager. `GET …/workspace/backlog/due-reviews` lists bets whose date has arrived
  and that have no verdict; `POST …/backlog/{taskId}/bet/review` closes or reschedules one; `list_team_backlog` takes
  `dueForReview`. Every task copy site (all status transitions, the cadence writeback, dependency rewrite on delete,
  handoff intake) now carries the bet. No bet fields = an ordinary task; stored tasks read unchanged.
- **Outcome events.** `POST/GET /groupstore/groups/{groupId}/outcomes` records a real-world result against a backlog
  task or an approval decision. Off until the group has an `outcomeConfig` (409 otherwise). Bounded per subject, per
  team and by age, idempotent by `idempotencyKey` (scoped to the team), the principal is the authenticated caller. Stored
  in `group_outcomes` on MongoDB and PostgreSQL ([`IOutcomeEventStore`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/groups/outcomes/IOutcomeEventStore.java)),
  cascaded on permanent group deletion, pseudonymized on GDPR erasure. The RETRO phase quotes the newest
  `retroDigestSize` outcomes as labelled data; `GET /hitl/analytics/outcomes` joins approval decisions with them per category.
- **Manager.** Workspace page: *File as a bet*, the bet on each backlog row, a *Bets due for review* section with Mark
  reviewed / Reschedule, per-task *Outcomes*, a *What came of approvals* table and the outcome setting on the group
  config panel. `groupLearning.*` in all 11 locales.
- **Platform Operator.** Read-only grants for the outcomes, due-reviews and outcome-analytics endpoints and a prompt
  section; **revision 56** (above the open #1245's 55). Deliberately not granted: recording an outcome or a bet verdict.

### Design decisions

- Outcomes are team-scoped and never decide anything: no promotion, demotion or re-gating reads them.
- `outcomeConfig == null` is *off*, not a default (unlike `retroConfig`): the endpoint refuses and the retro is unchanged.
- A task id joins an approval decision only through a `TASK` unit, so an unrelated row that carries the same id is not
  credited.
- A bet is due whatever the task's status.

### Not in this PR

Cross-team lesson sharing (explicit grants) and the scheduled, cost-capped consolidation of retro lessons are a second
PR. Not run locally: the `*IT` store tests (Docker) and the Playwright suite; CI is the gate for those.

Docs: [`docs/team-learning-loop.md`](../team-learning-loop.md).
- Final Platform Operator revision for 6.7.0 after merging main, #1259, #1260 and #1265 into this branch: **60** (the revisions 55–59 named in those PRs' fragments and earlier on this branch were intermediate).
- Platform Operator: closes the remaining gaps of the ten 6.7 follow-up notes; final revision 60. The prompt now names the
  oversight deployment properties (sampling switch, retentions, maintenance interval, suspension cache TTL), the served
  `approvalContext` shape and its render outcomes, human task results under TASK-granularity approval, the free human
  tie-break, Slack's edit limits and `hitlShareRuleDrafts`, reason-code and category rules, `hitl.edit.refused`, the
  human-only backlog delete, and the tool-call guardrails' `disclosure` parameters, could-not-decide reasons, trace step
  and budget accounting.

## ✨ feat(groups): team lessons - sharing between teams and cost-capped consolidation (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/team-learning-lessons`), stacked on `feat/team-learning-loop` (PR #1264), which sits on the unmerged
`feat/operator-mcp-integration`

### What changed and why

A team's retro lessons were visible only inside their own group, FIFO-evicted at `maxStoredLessons` and never consolidated.
Both are now possible, **off unless the group has a `lessonConfig`**:

- **Cross-team lessons.** `lessonConfig.shareWith` (owner) and `readFrom` (reader), consent on both sides like a handoff;
  `GET /groupstore/groups/{groupId}/lessons` and `…/lessons/shared` (which also returns `notShared`, the owners that did not
  grant). The reading team's RETRO phase quotes the shared lessons after its input, bounded, labelled as other teams' words
  (the hook added for outcomes in #1264 now composes both).
- **Lesson consolidation.** [`LessonConsolidationService`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/runtime/internal/LessonConsolidationService.java)
  merges lessons that say the same thing and retires a lesson a later outcome contradicted. It rides the schedule machinery
  (`ScheduleFireExecutor` dispatches on the `lessonType` marker, as for Dream and team cadences; `PUT/DELETE
  …/lessons/consolidation/schedule`), reuses Dream's summarization call and cost arithmetic, and checks `maxCostPerRun` **before**
  the call against a worst case (dropping lessons until it fits, making no call when it cannot). A retirement must cite outcome
  ids the model was shown. Every change is conditional on the lesson being unchanged; the removed text, reason and evidence go
  to the new `group_lesson_history` store (MongoDB and PostgreSQL) first. `POST …/lessons/consolidation/run?dryRun=true`
  previews.
- **Manager.** A *Team lessons* card on the workspace page: own lessons, shared lessons with the owners that did not grant,
  consolidation state with schedule / preview / run, and the history; a line on the group config panel. `groupLessons.*` in all
  11 locales.
- **Platform Operator.** Read-only grants for the four lesson reads and a prompt section; **revision 55**. Not granted:
  scheduling, running or removing the consolidation (it rewrites a team's memory and spends budget).

### Design decisions

- A separate service rather than extending `DreamService`: Dream consolidates one *user's* memories, these are a *team's*, and
  the grouping, the evidence requirement and the history differ. The call and the cost arithmetic are shared.
- The cap is a hard pre-check, not Dream's soft "checked before each call". One call per run makes the soft form a no-op.
- Retirement without evidence is refused rather than trusted: a model asserting a lesson is wrong is not an outcome.
- History is a store of its own, not a flag on the memory entry, so the live set stays what members recall.

### Not in this PR

FIFO eviction in `RetroEngine` still drops the oldest lesson without recording it in the history. Sharing is read-only: a reader
cannot write to another team's lessons.

Docs: [`docs/team-learning-loop.md`](../team-learning-loop.md#lessons).

### Review follow-ups

- `RestScheduleStore` refuses the `lessonType=lesson_consolidation` marker in a create or update body (an update may only
  echo its own row's marker), and an update of a stored consolidation schedule needs EDIT on its group. A forged marker
  let anyone who could create a schedule consolidate a team they cannot edit. ZIP import goes through the same guard; the
  MCP tool cannot set metadata.
- A merge rewrites the kept lesson with `replaceIfUnchanged` instead of an unconditional `upsert`; losing that race skips
  the merge (`skipped_changed`) and drops no duplicate.
- A permanent group delete also removes the `lesson-consolidation-<groupId>` schedule.
- The workspace page shows the lessons panel from the group's current version, not the URL's.
- Docs: `maxCostPerRun` is an estimate, not a hard ceiling (the answer length is not enforced); history keeps the newest
  1,000 rows per team; an owner absent from both shared lists may still have lessons outside the cap.

### Review follow-ups (second round)

- Merged `feat/team-learning-loop` (which had `main` merged in): the full-metrics dashboard takes the base layout with the
  lesson-consolidation panel beside the vote tie-break panel; operator **revision 58** (the base, after a second merge
  that brought in the operator MCP integration, is at 57).
- `lessonConfig.consolidation.parameters` follows the SERVICE judge's vault rule (`ServiceJudgeVaultGrants`): a
  `${vault:...}` must name a secret granted to all agents. Checked at group save and again before each run's model call.
- `RestGroupLessons.schedule` applies `eddi.schedule.min-interval-seconds` and answers an unsatisfiable cron
  (`0 0 30 2 *`) with 400 instead of 500.
- A retirement or a merge's duplicate drop whose conditional delete loses the race is no longer reported as applied; a
  partial merge names the duplicate it kept.
- `RestScheduleStore.fireNow` requires EDIT on a consolidation schedule's group before the owner checks; an update may
  not rename a consolidation schedule (its name is how the group's endpoints find it).
- Manager: a SKIPPED run toasts a warning with the reason instead of "finished"; the schedule control sends a time zone
  (the viewer's by default) and the state badge shows the stored one; each section shows an error state when its read
  fails, instead of the empty or "not configured" text.

## 🧪 test(cluster): multi-process end-to-end cluster suite, nightly cluster demo, soak and scale harness (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI (`test/cluster-e2e-evidence`)

### What changed and why

The [cluster readiness review](../../planning/cluster-production-readiness-review.md) (committed with this
branch) found that nothing ran more than one JVM against a real database and a real NATS, with no
fault injection, no soak and no scale test, and that `scripts/cluster-demo` never ran in CI. This
is its Phase 3 ("evidence"), written against the contract of the Phase 1 fixes rather than against
today's behaviour.

- **End-to-end suite** (`src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/integration/cluster`). `ClusterHarness` starts three
  EDDI processes from the packaged app (the production Dockerfile, as `ContainerBaseIT` builds it), an
  R3 NATS cluster (`--no_advertise`, so clients stay behind the proxy), MongoDB or PostgreSQL, the
  demo's mock LLM, and a Toxiproxy proxy per (EDDI node, NATS server). The 8 scenarios in
  `AbstractClusterE2EIT` cover ordering without faults, latency, a partitioned node in
  `degraded.turns=reject`, NATS replies dropped after the write was applied (A2), a half-open link
  (A1), a holder frozen by SIGSTOP past `lease.ttl` (A1/A6), repeated stream-leader step-downs under
  load (A2/D3), and `kill -9` mid-turn. Each scenario writes JSON evidence to
  `target/cluster-e2e/<db>/`. A scenario that `main` is expected to fail names the finding in its
  title, and its assertions state the contract.
- **`ConversationHistoryChecker`**: a history checker in the style of Knossos and Elle, reduced to the
  ordering contract. It compares client answers, committed history and the LLM's view, and flags
  lost, duplicated, phantom, refused-but-committed, out-of-order (real-time order), overlapping and
  stale-context turns. It has 20 unit tests of its own in `ConversationHistoryCheckerTest`, which run
  in the normal build.
- **Opt-in.** The suite runs only with `-Deddi.cluster.e2e=true`, so `./mvnw verify -DskipITs=false`
  skips it. It runs through surefire, which has no 15-minute fork timeout and no rerun.
- **`.github/workflows/cluster-e2e-nightly.yml`**: the suite on MongoDB and on PostgreSQL, nightly and
  on PRs that touch `engine/cluster/**`. The cluster demo runs nightly on both databases. Results and
  node logs are uploaded as artifacts, with a job summary. The workflow is not a required check.
- **`scripts/cluster-demo/demo.py`**: `--exit-code`, so CI sees a failed scenario, and `--summary-md`,
  for the step summary.
- **`scripts/loadtest/cluster_soak.py`**: `soak` runs chunked steady load for a configurable duration,
  restarting replicas along the way. It samples per-node heap, threads, held leases and open files,
  plus NATS subscriptions and stream and bucket growth, and fits a per-hour slope to each series.
  `scale` runs many concurrently held leases per node, locally or against a cluster deployed on
  separate hosts (`--external`). Both are documented in the load-test README.
- **[Clustering](../clustering.md#how-cluster-mode-is-tested)**: a new section, "How cluster mode is
  tested".

### First local run, on `main` at `1e8ecd372` (Docker Desktop, Windows)

On both databases, 6 of the 8 scenarios pass: ordering, latency, partition, SIGSTOP past the TTL,
stream-leader failover and `kill -9`. The other two fail as the review predicted.

- **A2**: the holder's healthy turn is answered 409 after 7 s of dropped NATS replies. The next turn
  waits about 10–13 s.
- **A1**: the holder on a half-open link answers 200 for a turn the takeover fenced off. The checker
  reports it as `LOST`.

On the SIGSTOP scenario, the frozen holder correctly answers 409 after it thaws.

### What's next

Rerun once the A–D workstreams land. The two scenarios that name a finding should then pass.

### Carried into this PR for 6.7.0

- The branch-coverage tests of the closed #1281 (unit tests only, no production change) bring the merged JaCoCo branch ratio back over the 0.80 gate; two of them were adapted to the grant-visibility rule of #1249.
- `NatsTlsHandshakeTest.presentsTheClientCertificateToABrokerThatRequiresMutualTls` is quarantined (`@Disabled`): on the Linux CI runner it intermittently sees the connection CLOSED, while it passes locally. Client-certificate presentation stays covered by the JVM-default-keystore handshake test and `NatsConnectionSecurityIT`; the root cause is a 6.8 item.
- The NATS fixture password of `ClusterHarness` is a low-entropy string so secret scanning stays quiet.

## 🔒 fix(backup): agent-findings review — repeated references, parser references, export vetting (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/agent-findings-backup`)

### What changed and why

Verified the 43 CodeRabbit "agent findings" on backup / import / export / sync against the
current code. 18 had been scanned before the review fixes of 2026-10-31 and most of those were
already closed there; the rest were checked on today's code.

**One document, one copy, one request**

- The ZIP and remote source readers read and parse a workflow once per id and version, however
  often the source agent names it; every further position shares that record
  (`IResourceSource.atPosition`). `UpgradeExecutor` now repoints a copy of an adopted source
  config (`copyOf`) instead of the shared one.
- The ZIP reader reads an extension file once per archive path; both readers scan a parser
  document for its dictionaries once, not once per step naming it.
- The remote reader remembers failed reads, so a repeated failing reference is not requested
  again, and stops after `eddi.backup.sync.max-requests` requests (default `10000`) with `502`.
- Export reads each referenced resource once (`IResourceId` has no value equality, so the map kept
  one copy per reference); the legacy merge preview scans each archived workflow once.
- Import: two spellings of one workflow URI are one workflow; versions of one id, which read the
  same archive file, are decoded and written once and every reference names the one resource;
  a workflow directory holding more than one workflow document is a `400`.
- `StructuralMatcher` indexes source extensions by id instead of scanning them per diff row.
- Export finds `${connection:…}` references with the linear `ConnectionReference.findAll`
  instead of the backtracking regex.

**Authorization**

- Export no longer counts an agent or group reference (checked for `USE` only when the workflow
  was written) as vetting the id it carries, so a configuration named elsewhere under that id
  needs its own `VIEW`.
- A parser document created by import or upgrade runs `RestParserStore`'s reference check: every
  dictionary it names needs `VIEW` (`WorkflowReferenceAccess.requireAccessToParserReferences`).
- A structural preview's REMOVE row names a target workflow only for a caller with `VIEW` on it.
- A failed merge's rollback puts back the name, description, origin and sync baseline it rewrote
  onto the *current* descriptor, so a sharing change the owner made meanwhile is not undone.

**Secrets**

- `ScrubbedSecrets` compares the destination fields of both sides: a source that drops the
  target's `baseUrl` (provider default) or carries a non-string `host` counts as a changed
  destination, and the target's literal credential is not restored into it.
- `SecretScrubber` judges a header or query map serialized into a string (an LLM task's
  `customHeaders` / `customQueryParams`) entry by entry under the map's name, as the editor read
  does, so an opaque credential header no longer leaves in an export.

**Verdicts** (43 findings):

| Verdict | Count | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| FIXED | 24 | this branch |
| ALREADY_FIXED | 16 | the 2026-10-31 backup review fixes (#1189) |
| ACCEPTED_RISK | 2 | DNS rebinding between check and connect (documented in `security.md`); `VIEW` on an agent covering its step references (documented in `import-export-an-agent.md`) |
| NEEDS_DECISION | 1 | per-name connection lookups on export |

### Design decisions

- Repeated references are collapsed, not refused: an agent naming a workflow twice and a stale
  parser document naming an older dictionary version are legitimate and keep importing.
- The request budget is a count, configurable, far above any real agent; an elapsed-time budget
  for the whole sync was not added (each request already has its own 30 s timeout).
- Not done: export resolves each distinct `${connection:…}` name through
  `IConnectionStore.idOfName` + `readByName`, each of which reads every connection; a batched
  lookup needs a store API change (left as a decision).

**Files:** [`IResourceSource.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/backup/IResourceSource.java),
[`RemoteApiResourceSource.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/backup/impl/RemoteApiResourceSource.java),
[`ZipResourceSource.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/backup/impl/ZipResourceSource.java),
[`RestExportService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/backup/impl/RestExportService.java),
[`RestImportService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/backup/impl/RestImportService.java),
[`UpgradeExecutor.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/backup/impl/UpgradeExecutor.java),
[`StructuralMatcher.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/backup/impl/StructuralMatcher.java),
[`ScrubbedSecrets.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/backup/impl/ScrubbedSecrets.java),
[`WorkflowReferenceAccess.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/backup/impl/WorkflowReferenceAccess.java),
[`SecretScrubber.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/sanitize/SecretScrubber.java),
[`import-export-an-agent.md`](../import-export-an-agent.md), [`agent-sync-guide.md`](../agent-sync-guide.md),
[`configuration-reference.md`](../configuration-reference.md)

## 🐛 fix(configs,datastore): agent findings for the configuration stores and the datastore (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/agent-findings-configs-datastore`)

Verified the 56 CodeRabbit "agent findings" for `configs/**`, `datastore/mongo`, `datastore/postgres` and
`ui/BrandedOpenApiRoute` against `main`. Many had been closed since the scans, mostly by #1191
(`87f98b7b3`), #1190, #1175, #1173 and #1161. Verdicts: 14 fixed here, 29 already fixed, 5 accepted risk,
8 needing a decision, 0 false positives (two of the already-fixed ones left a gap that is fixed here too).

### What changed and why

**Reference checks.**
- `RestWorkflowStore.addReference` now needs only a host, not a scheme. A scheme-relative
  `//ai.labs.output/outputstore/outputsets/<id>` was skipped, but the engine resolves a step by host and
  id and loaded it anyway.
- A parser document's own `config.uri` is now one of the references `RestParserStore` checks. The
  standalone parse endpoint follows it (`InputParserTask.loadParserDocument`) and uses that parser's
  dictionaries.
- `RestAction` and `RestExpression` (the Manager's action/expression helpers) check VIEW on every rule
  set, API-call set, output set and dictionary they read, not just on the workflow. They pick a store by
  step type and read whatever id `config.uri` spells, relative ones included. A resource the caller
  cannot read contributes nothing.

**Artifact validators (group conversations).**
- A JSON-schema validator is refused at save, and not run at write, when its evaluation is unbounded
  (`ArtifactSchemaComplexity`). The library evaluates every `$ref` separately and reads no clock.
  Measured: 20 `$defs` that each `allOf` the previous one twice took 2 s to save and 3.6 s per artifact
  write, doubling with each further level. A self-`allOf` threw a `StackOverflowError` out of the tool
  call, and two routes to the same property doubled the work per nesting level. The check refuses:
  - an expansion beyond 2000 subschemas;
  - recursion that comes back at the same content location;
  - two recursive routes that do not start with differently named properties.

  Trees and binary trees still pass. A nested `$id` is refused rather than resolved by guesswork.
- `REGEX` validators and schema `pattern`s are run through `BoundedRegex.excessiveRepetition`, the scan
  agent tests already use. A counted repetition of an empty match (`(?:(?:){99999}){99999}`) spins
  without reading input, so the match deadline never saw it.

**JSON Patch.** `ConfigJsonPatcher` masks credentials and judges their relocation by the store's own
read rules. Before, it used the generic redaction rules, while `RestLlmStore`'s read leaves
`tasks[].toolCacheScopes` as it is. A key copied to `toolCacheScopes.apiKey` was accepted as "still
hidden" and then returned in plain text. The LLM and API-call stores pass their `NOT_CONFIGURATION` skip
paths through a new `RestVersionInfo.patch` overload.

**Knowledge bases.**
- A 6.5.0 name-layout knowledge base that changes its `storeType`, or the server it connects to, moves
  to the id layout. The server parameters are `host`, `port`, `database`, `databaseName`,
  `connectionString`, `serverUrl`, `baseUrl` and `tenantName`. On the new backend or server the same
  name maps to that one's default store, which can belong to another legacy knowledge base with the
  same name.
- The standalone endpoints (search, ingest, documents, chunks) apply the deployment rule: the caller
  must belong to every space tenant the configuration's `${vars:<tenant>/…}` / `${vault:<tenant>/…}`
  references name, with variables expanded and connections followed. They build the model and store on
  the spot with the server's authority, and no deployment ever checked them. This adds
  `SpaceReferenceGuard.requireMayUse` and `VaultGrantChecker.referencedTenantsOf`. Docs:
  [RAG](../rag.md).

**Connections.** `ConnectionStore.readByName` reads the live version of each connection. Before, it
read the version the descriptor names. The response filter advances that descriptor only after
`updateConnection` has invalidated the registry, so a lookup in between cached the previous
`allowedHosts` for the registry's one-minute TTL.

**Datastore.** `GridFsAttachmentStore` counts quota usage, and enumerates a conversation's blobs for
erasure, on the primary, like `MongoScheduleStore`'s erasure reads. The client reads `nearest`, so a
lagging secondary undercounted admitted uploads and left committed blobs out of an erasure.

**API description.** `quarkus.http.auth.permission.api-docs.paths` now lists `/openapi.json`, `.yaml`,
`.yml` and their `/q/` forms. Neither `/openapi` nor `/openapi/*` matches them, so the role-gated
document was served to any authenticated token through a suffix URL. Docs:
[security](../security.md), [configuration reference](../configuration-reference.md).

### Design decisions

- **Schema complexity is bounded statically.** networknt offers no hook that runs during evaluation, so
  a budget cannot be enforced while the schema runs. The analysis follows only references inside the
  document. References to other documents are left to the loader, which loads nothing but the bundled
  meta-schemas.
- **Standalone RAG reuses the deployment rule** (`SpaceReferenceGuard`) instead of a second
  tenant-membership check.

### Needs a decision (not changed)

- Descriptor `PATCH` against sharing: the full descriptor is written back, guarded only by the resource
  version, so a concurrent revocation can be undone. Needs a descriptor revision or field-level writes
  on both backends.
- Mongo authorization reads use `nearest`. Moving descriptor reads to the primary affects every
  descriptor read.
- Slack approval records are keyed by the integration's name, not its id.
- A non-admin `duplicateLlm` (or a deep workflow copy) carries plaintext credentials the copier cannot
  read.
- The knowledge-base collision check does not see locations used by older, still-pinned versions.
- Uploads are not fenced against a concurrent conversation erasure (MongoDB and PostgreSQL).

### Follow-up: review findings on the parse path and the connection lookup

- **Standalone parse checks a parser document's `config.uri` per request.** Parser documents saved
  before the save-time check can still name another owner's parser, and `RestSemanticParser` loaded
  them unchecked. The cache entry now records the parser documents `config.uri` named when it was
  built, and every request requires `VIEW` on each of them on the request thread, hit or miss, with the
  same rule `RestParserStore` applies on save. The cache stays shared and the document is still read
  once per cached parser. The parser is now resolved on the request thread instead of the pool thread,
  so that the check has the caller's identity. Not done: a one-off scan or migration of stored legacy
  parsers, and the same per-request check for dictionary references in `extensions`.
- **The connection lookup reads from the primary.** `IResourceStorage.primaryReads()` returns a view
  served by the authoritative copy: on MongoDB the same collections with `ReadPreference.primary()`,
  on PostgreSQL the storage itself. `ConnectionStore` resolves the current version and fetches the
  connection through it, so a lagging secondary cannot hand the registry a connection whose
  `allowedHosts` was just narrowed. Every other store keeps `nearest`. Authorization descriptor reads
  are unchanged and remain the open decision above.
